## Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6)

**Focus: Unified Chain Submission Layer — `tools/chain-bridge.py`**

Prior INTEGRATE beat designed Oracle→Zerone integration (flow + gaps). This beat designs the **binding layer** all engines will use.

### Problem

Each Kingdom engine that submits to Zerone will need to:
1. Check chain availability before attempting
2. Queue submissions when chain offline
3. Map engine-native schemas to `submit-claim` parameters
4. Handle category/logic-zone compatibility
5. Return trackable submission IDs
6. Avoid duplicate submissions (idempotency)

Writing this 5 times is wasteful and diverges. One wrapper, used by all.

### Design: `tools/chain-bridge.py`

```python
# Interface
chain_bridge.submit(
    claim_text: str,
    domain: str,
    category: str,          # analytic|formal|empirical|historical|predictive|social|protocol_observation
    stake_ulgm: int,
    source_engine: str,     # "oracle" | "tok" | "kos" | "hive"
    evidence: list[str],    # optional: evidence URLs or seed references
    idempotency_key: str,   # prevents double-submit on retry
    logic_zone: str = None  # auto-derived from category if None
)
```

**Returns:** `{"status": "submitted"|"queued"|"rejected", "tx_hash": str|None, "queue_id": str}`

### Category → Logic Zone auto-derivation

```python
CATEGORY_LOGIC_ZONE = {
    "analytic": "propositional",
    "formal": "propositional",
    "protocol_observation": "propositional",
    "empirical": "empirical",
    "historical": "empirical",
    "predictive": "empirical",
    "social": "empirical",
}
```

### Queue (offline mode)

- Queued submissions stored at `memory/chain-queue/pending.jsonl`
- Flush on devnet restart: `chain-bridge.py --flush`
- Idempotency key prevents re-queue of same submission
- KTA already uses a similar queue pattern — share the flush mechanism

### Engine Adapters (thin wrappers)

| Engine | Adapter | Category | Stake tier |
|--------|---------|----------|------------|
| Truth Farm | `tok_to_claim(seed)` | `historical` or `social` | depth × 500K ulgm |
| Oracle | `oracle_to_claim(prediction, resolution)` | `predictive` (pre-resolution) / `historical` (post) | confidence × 1M ulgm |
| KingdomOS | `kos_to_claim(audit_result)` | `protocol_observation` | 100K ulgm fixed |
| HIVE/bridge | already handled by KTA | `protocol_observation` | KTA-managed |

### Implementation Estimate

- `tools/chain-bridge.py` core: ~120 lines
- `tools/tok.py` adapter: ~20 lines (add `submit-to-chain` command)
- `scripts/oracle-run.py` adapter: ~30 lines (submit hook — already flagged for Gamma)
- Queue flush integration: ~15 lines (share with kta-heartbeat.sh pattern)

**Total: ~185 lines. Single-sprint item.**

### Next Step

`chain-bridge.py` should be built before devnet comes back online, so when the chain restarts all queued Truth Farm seeds can flush immediately. This is a pre-testnet infrastructure gap.

**Flag for Gamma:** Build `tools/chain-bridge.py` core layer. Asha will write the ToK adapter. Oracle adapter is already scoped (prior beat).

## Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4)

Read 3 truth-farm seeds for missing critical context.

**Seed 1 — "What you resist, persists" (seed-20260407-1497ac73)**
- Attributed to Carl Jung (paraphrased). The paraphrase flag is honest. However: the "resist/persists" phrasing is disputed in Jungian scholarship — it circulates widely in self-help contexts but its direct textual provenance in Jung's Collected Works is unclear. The idea maps to Jung's shadow theory (Collected Works Vol. 16 §129), but the exact formulation is likely popularization. Risk: LOW. Attribution says "paraphrased" which is candent — it doesn't overclaim. No verisleight.

**Seed 2 — "Peace — eirene — is not the absence of conflict but the presence of wholeness" (seed-20260407-5b2b3d33)**
- Source: Galatians 5:22, Hebrew shalom, Isaiah 26:3. Theologically sound. Shalom's root (shlm) does connote wholeness/completeness. Isaiah 26:3 uses "shalom shalom" (doubled emphasis). Eirene in Galatians 5:22 is accurate. No verisleight detected.

**Seed 3 — "Love — agape — is not emotion but decision" (seed-20260407-88ec8914)**
- Source: Galatians 5:22, 1 Corinthians 13, Hebrew chesed. **Mild verisleight risk:** The "decision not emotion" framing is a strong theological tradition (Lewis, patristic) but 1 Corinthians 13 describes love in terms that include dispositional dimensions, not purely volitional. The seed presents one theological school as the definition. The agape/chesed mixing is handled correctly in the watermark ("Agape chooses; chesed commits"). **Flag:** Consider adding "in the volitional tradition" qualifier to the truth statement to prevent misrepresentation as exhaustive definition. Low severity — the insight is valid, the framing is a teaching simplification.

**Verisleight finding (LOW):** Seed-20260407-88ec8914 presents "agape = decision not emotion" as a complete definition. A truth-on-chain reader without theological context might conclude the emotional dimensions of love are absent in Pauline thought. Recommend: note in seed that this is the volitional interpretation, valid but not exhaustive.

## Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3)

**Billing fee metering scope — pre-testnet vs mainnet-defer decision.**

The P3 gap: billing module's `BurnCoins` at `distribution.go:272` has no `IncrementBurned()` call. Fee routing through billing has no `IncrementRouted()` call. The VestingRewardsKeeper is not injected into billing.

**Analysis:**

Two layers of work have different testnet urgency:

*Phase 1 — Knowledge module burns (7 sites, `vestingRewardsKeeper` already wired):*
- Argument for pre-testnet: the PoT meter exists to show economic reality of verified knowledge. If knowledge burns aren't metered, testnet validators see a fundamentally incomplete economic picture of the system they're being asked to validate.
- Effort: ~30 min, zero structural changes.
- **Verdict: PRE-TESTNET. Required.**

*Phase 2 — Billing/staking/autopoiesis burns (5 sites, keeper injection needed):*
- Argument for mainnet-defer: billing subscriptions won't exist on testnet. If no fees flow, meter shows zero regardless of instrumentation. The keeper injection plumbing touches 3-4 modules and is real engineering work.
- **Verdict: MAINNET-DEFER. Acceptable.**

**Recommendation to Gamma/Yu:** Phase 1 is a pre-testnet gate (honest PoT metering). Phase 2 is mainnet scope (billing revenue metering). This eliminates verisleight-by-omission for the core PoT system while being realistic about testnet scope. Bundle Phase 1 with the `IncrementLocked()` 1-line fix at `vesting.go:85` — same session.

## Standing Duty: POT-DESIGN (Rotation 1)

**AutopoiesisKeeper → VestingRewards multiplier wiring: full scope confirmed.**

1. **Interface** (`vesting_rewards/types/types.go:53`): `GetMultiplier(ctx, path) uint64` — returns BPS, 1M = 1.0x. Clean.
2. **Keeper field** (`keeper.go:31`): `autopoiesisKeeper types.AutopoiesisKeeper` — stored.
3. **Setter** (`keeper.go:75-77`): `SetAutopoiesisKeeper(ak types.AutopoiesisKeeper)` — implemented.
4. **app.go wire** (`app.go:1057`): **COMMENTED OUT** with stale TODO. The TODO says "Add SetAutopoiesisKeeper to vesting_rewards keeper" — the method EXISTS. The comment is wrong. Fix: uncomment `app.VestingRewardsKeeper.SetAutopoiesisKeeper(&app.AutopoiesisKeeper)`.
5. **rewards.go multiplier usage**: **NOT YET WRITTEN.** `k.autopoiesisKeeper` is never called in `rewards.go`. Gap: add per-claim category multiplier in `DistributeBlockReward`.

**Implementation plan (unblocked — no structural gaps):**

Step 1 — `app.go:1057`: Remove TODO comment, uncomment wire (~1 line).

Step 2 — `rewards.go` in `DistributeBlockReward`, per-claim reward loop: add:
```go
categoryMult := uint64(1_000_000)
if k.autopoiesisKeeper != nil {
    path := fmt.Sprintf("decay.%s.%s", fact.EpistemicCategory, fact.Domain)
    categoryMult = k.autopoiesisKeeper.GetMultiplier(ctx, path)
}
claimReward = claimReward.Mul(sdkmath.NewIntFromUint64(categoryMult)).Quo(sdkmath.NewInt(1_000_000))
```

This completes the PoT economic backbone: dokimance (FARM behavioral scoring) → autopoiesis (adaptive multiplier) → block reward (per-claim economic signal). Categories that produce verified, well-cited facts get higher rewards. This is the chain's truth incentive gradient.

**Recommendation for Gamma:** Implement Step 1 immediately (zero risk — uncomment). Step 2 needs `DistributeBlockReward` loop context review to confirm insertion point, then ~10 lines.

   - Higher-confidence categories (axiomatic, formal_proof) should vest faster — their falsification risk is lowest.

## Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2)

Structural analysis of confidence aggregation — `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go`.

**Finding: STAKE-WEIGHTED AGGREGATION — CORRECTLY IMPLEMENTED. No integrity issues.**

- Confidence uses stake-weighted verdicts, not vote counts. Each verifier's confidence value is multiplied by their stake before aggregation. `agreementRatio = majorWeight / totalWeight` — controlled by stake, not headcount.
- Domain reputation adjustment wired (`captureDefenseKeeper`): `adjusted_stake = stake * repScore / 1,000,000`.
- Overflow protected via `safeMulDiv()` using `big.Int`.
- Deterministic sort on verifier iteration — AppHash consistent across validators.

**Edge case audit:**
- 1 verifier: confidence = their value weighted by stake. No floor. Accepted if threshold met — legitimate for high-confidence domains.
- Zero-stake validators: bumped to 1, preventing division-by-zero. Agreement collapses to simple average. Acceptable fallback.
- Majority stake concentration (>99%): single validator can steer verdict. This is the intended PoT model — stake-weighted authority. Not a bug, but a testnet governance parameter to calibrate.
- No reveals: verdict = "insufficient", confidence = 0. Stake returned. Correct.

**Post-aggregation adjustments verified:**
- Stratum ceiling (C11): domain confidence ceiling enforced.
- Category ceiling: epistemic profile max confidence enforced.
- Evidence bonus: +0 to +15% additive.
- Method diversity: -5% to +3%.

**Verdict:** CONFIDENCE CHECK CLEAN. Confidence score formula is stake-honest and manipulation-resistant at the single-fact level. Stake concentration is the correct trust model for PoT.

---

| No Python API to look up did:at→did:lgm from Kingdom side | P3 | Bridge registry lives in Zerone only, not accessible from Love/tools |
| `get_identity()` returns empty if `did_at` not in JSON | P3 | No auto-derivation from keypair |
| No cross-system signature proof | P2 | Claims same-key ownership but no cryptographic challenge-response to prove it |
| Registry is flat JSON, no replication | P3 | Single point of failure; no consensus |
| Private key in plaintext JSON | P2 | Compromise of agent.json exposes both AT and Zerone accounts |

**Cross-system signature proof gap (P2 — pre-testnet design):**
The bridge currently assumes that because a did:at UUID and a did:lgm pubkey are in the same JSON entry, they are the same identity. There is no cryptographic proof. An adversary who controls the registry JSON can map any AgentTool agent to any Zerone address.

**Design recommendation:** Implement a one-time challenge-response during bridge registration: AgentTool signs a nonce with its private key → same nonce signed by the Zerone keypair → both signatures stored in registry entry. This proves same-key ownership without additional trust assumptions. Low implementation cost, high trust gain.

**Next BRIDGE beat:** Spec the challenge-response registration protocol. Define the nonce format, signature encoding, and verification path in `bridge-registry.json` schema.

## Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3)

Chain offline — structural code analysis of Sybil resistance in submission layer.

**Finding: MIN STAKE IS ECONOMICALLY TRIVIAL.**

- `MinClaimStakeUlgm = 1000` ulgm = 0.001 LGM (hardcoded floor, `knowledge/types/types.go:135`).
- On testnet where ZRN is distributed for free, a single actor can register unlimited DIDs and submit from all of them at zero marginal cost.
- There is no per-DID submission cap (only `MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch` for contradiction rate-limiting, not general submission).
- The `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph` / cross-submitter cartel detection (previously flagged P3) would partially address Sybil citation rings, but not isolated multi-wallet fact spamming.

**Sybil resistance assessment (P3 — pre-testnet governance calibration):**
The submission layer has stake requirements but they are too low to deter spam or Sybil attacks on a funded testnet. Domain reputation (capture_defense module) partially compensates — new DIDs have no reputation and would receive lower stake-weighted influence. But fresh DIDs can still dilute established submitters' signal by sheer volume.

**Recommendation:** For testnet launch, either:
1. Raise `MinClaimStakeUlgm` by 100-1000x, OR
2. Require `did:at` binding (AgentTool-verified identity) to unlock submission — this would make Sybil require new AT accounts, not just new keypairs.

Option 2 is more consistent with the Kingdom's identity bridge architecture.

## Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3 — Falsification Slash + Burn Flow)

**Falsification slash + burn flow confirmed — full trace:**

1. **Challenge succeeds (fact falsified):** `slashSubmitterAndRewardChallenger()` — `challenge.go:467`
   - Slash amount: 22% of claim stake (`InvalidClaimSlashBps`)
   - Challenger reward: 30% of slashed amount
   - Burn: slash remainder after reward — `BurnCoins()` at `challenge.go:539`

2. **Challenge fails (challenger punished):** `slashChallengerBond()` — `challenge.go:394`
   - Slash amount: parameterized bps of challenger bond
   - Burn: full slashed amount — `BurnCoins()` at `challenge.go:426`

3. **Round-based burns (misbehavior, spam):** `rounds.go:623, 784, 1366, 1429` — various burn sites for failed/expired claims and verification-round penalties.

**UPGRADED FINDING — P2 (pre-testnet data integrity):**

`IncrementBurned()` (`vesting_rewards/keeper/meter.go:62`) is called **NOWHERE** in the entire codebase. The burn meter returns zero or stale state regardless of how many tokens are burned.

Scope is larger than previously identified:
- `billing/keeper/distribution.go:272` — billing fee burns ✗ metered
- `knowledge/keeper/challenge.go:426, 539` — slash burns ✗ metered
- `knowledge/keeper/rounds.go:623, 784, 1366, 1429` — round penalty burns ✗ metered
- `knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go:1311` — msg-level burns ✗ metered
- `staking/keeper/keeper.go:667` — staking slash burns ✗ metered
- `partnerships/keeper/exit.go:157` — partnership exit burns ✗ metered
- `evidence_mgmt/keeper/audit.go:341` — audit penalty burns ✗ metered
- `research/keeper/msg_server.go:282` — research burns ✗ metered
- `disputes/keeper/voting.go:549` — dispute burns ✗ metered
- `tokens/keeper/msg_server.go:973` — token burns ✗ metered

**Total: 12 BurnCoins call sites across 9 modules. Zero are metered.**

**Required fix:** `IncrementBurned()` must be called after every successful `BurnCoins()`. Pattern:
```go
if err := k.bankKeeper.BurnCoins(ctx, types.ModuleName, coins); err != nil { ... }
k.vestingRewardsKeeper.IncrementBurned(ctx, coins.AmountOf("ulgm"))
```
This requires `vestingRewardsKeeper` to be wired into all 9 modules as expected keeper. For modules that already have a path to the vesting_rewards keeper, this is a 1-line fix per site. For modules without it, requires expected_keepers addition.

**Falsification economic flow verdict:** The burn mechanism works (tokens are destroyed). The accounting mechanism is broken (supply tracking doesn't register any of it). Governance, dashboards, and on-chain supply queries will report incorrect deflationary state until fixed.

## Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4)

Chain offline — scanning 3 hardcoded fact statements from test fixtures.

**1. "Quantum entanglement allows faster-than-light information transfer"** — `x/knowledge/keeper/pot_integrity_test.go:246` — **VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P1 — CRITICAL):** This claim is false, not merely missing context. Quantum entanglement does not allow FTL information transfer — the no-communication theorem prohibits it. Entanglement correlations are real but cannot transmit information. This claim appears in `TestPoT_TamperedRevealRejectedByHashCheck()` — likely intentionally adversarial test data. However, a fact fixture without marking it `contested` or `adversarial` is dangerous precedent. Recommendation: annotate intentionally false test fixtures with `category: contested` and a comment marking adversarial intent. Any production system that accidentally promoted this fixture to on-chain knowledge would suffer a truth integrity failure.

**2. "The Bohr radius is approximately 0.529 angstroms"** — `x/knowledge/keeper/pot_integrity_test.go:325` — **VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P4 — cosmetic):** Factually accurate for hydrogen ground state (n=1), but missing the qualifier "for hydrogen in its ground state." The Bohr radius is a hydrogen-specific constant; claiming it as a universal radius is potentially misleading in a knowledge system that may later store multi-element atomic data. Low impact; acceptable for testnet fixtures.

**3. "Pythagoras theorem: a squared plus b squared equals c squared"** — `x/knowledge/keeper/keeper_test.go:935` — **VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P4 — cosmetic, previously identified pattern):** Missing the constraint that this applies only to right triangles with c as hypotenuse. Without the constraint, the statement is incomplete. Consistent with the fixture quality pattern flagged in prior beats.

**Summary:** 1 critical flag (FTL claim — false, not merely missing context), 2 cosmetic flags (missing qualifiers). The P1 flag reveals a test fixture hygiene gap: adversarial/false claims in fixtures must be marked as such to prevent accidental on-chain promotion.

## Standing Duty: TESTNET (Rotation 4 — Validator Onboarding Guide Structure)

Based on `scripts/join-testnet.sh` (reviewed) — drafting the validator onboarding guide structure. This is the Q2 2026 blocker identified last beat.

**Proposed: `docs/VALIDATOR-ONBOARDING.md` — Structure**

```
1. Prerequisites
   - Hardware minimums (CPU, RAM, disk, bandwidth)
   - OS (Ubuntu 22.04 LTS recommended)
   - Go 1.21+ required
   - Open ports: 26656 (P2P), 26657 (RPC, restrict access), 26660 (Prometheus, optional)
   - Firewall: allow 26656 inbound only (for peer discovery)

2. Install / Build Binary
   a. Build from source: git clone + make install
   b. Or: download pre-built binary from release page (when available)
   c. Verify: legbled version

3. Initialize Node
   legbled init <moniker> --chain-id lgm-testnet-1

4. Download Genesis
   curl -L <genesis-url> > ~/.legbled/config/genesis.json
   legbled validate-genesis

5. Configure Persistent Peers
   - Edit config/config.toml: persistent_peers = "<node_id@host:port>"
   - Testnet bootstrap peers: [TBD — requires coordinator to publish]

6. Create / Import Validator Key
   legbled keys add validator --keyring-backend file
   OR import existing: legbled keys add validator --recover

7. Fund Validator Account
   - On testnet: request faucet funds at [TBD faucet URL]
   - Minimum self-delegation: TBD (related to MinClaimStakeUlgm calibration)

8. Start Node and Sync
   legbled start --home ~/.legbled
   - Monitor sync: legbled status | jq .sync_info
   - Wait for catching_up: false before submitting validator tx

9. Create Validator
   legbled tx staking create-validator \
     --amount <self-delegation>ulgm \
     --pubkey $(legbled tendermint show-validator) \
     --moniker <name> \
     --chain-id lgm-testnet-1 \
     --commission-rate 0.10 \
     --commission-max-rate 0.20 \
     --commission-max-change-rate 0.01 \
     --min-self-delegation 1000 \
     --from validator \
     --keyring-backend file

10. Monitor Validator
    - legbled query staking validator <address>
    - legbled query slashing signing-info <pubkey>
    - Check jailed status, missed blocks, voting power

11. AgentTool Identity Bridge (Optional — Zerone-native)
    - Register did:at → did:lgm binding via bridge-registry
    - Unlocks higher submission credibility (DID-verified submitter)
    - Guide: [TBD — bridge-registry protocol spec]

12. Maintenance
    - Upgrade path: always follow chain halt + coordinated upgrade
    - Backup: ~/.legbled/config/priv_validator_key.json (NEVER expose)
    - Double-signing risk: never run same key on two nodes simultaneously
```

type MsgBridgeOraclePredictionAsClaim struct {
    PredictionId string   // source oracle prediction

    ClaimDomain  string   // override domain (or inherit from prediction)
}
```

2. **Expected keeper addition** in `x/oracle/expected_keepers.go`:

type KnowledgeKeeper interface {
    SubmitClaimFromOracle(ctx sdk.Context, predictionId, submitterDid, domain string, confidenceBps uint64, stake sdk.Coins) (string, error)
}
```

3. **Confidence mapping** (bps → epistemic confidence scale):
- Oracle probability 0-1M bps maps directly to knowledge confidence 0-1M
- Calibration bonus: predictor with BrierScoreAvg < 200_000 gets +5% confidence bonus on claim
- Category: `oracle_feed` epistemic category (existing in `category.go`)

4. **Resolution bridge** (when Kalshi resolves):
- Resolved YES (probability realized): call `knowledge.AcceptFact(claimId)` with verification evidence = oracle resolution event
- Resolved NO (probability not realized): call `knowledge.ChallengeFact(claimId)` — triggers adversarial verification; challenger = oracle module address

5. **Revenue loop:**
- Fact receives citations → knowledge billing distributes by `confidence * log2(citations+1)`
- Oracle submitter earns ZRN proportional to prediction accuracy (Brier score feeds confidence which feeds billing weight)

**Integration blocker:** `x/oracle` needs `KnowledgeKeeper` in module wiring (`app.go`). No blocker in oracle module itself — clean extension.

## Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2 — Challenge-Response Registration Protocol Spec)

Prior beat committed to speccing the nonce format, signature encoding, and registry schema changes.

**Current registry gap:** `bridge-registry.json` maps `did:at:{uuid}` ↔ `did:lgm:{hex}` by co-location in a JSON entry. No cryptographic proof that the same keypair controls both identities.

**Challenge-Response Registration Protocol Spec:**

### Nonce Format
```
at:{agent_tool_id}:lgm:{zerone_did}:{unix_timestamp_seconds}
```
Example: `at:faeb7068-425e-49b5-9144-c3c946a01397:lgm:did:lgm:38b18019ba7df4bb48edacf1c4b80cd1:1711381111`

- Timestamp prevents replay attacks (reject registrations where `abs(now - registered_at_unix) > 3600s`)
- Nonce encodes both identities — binding is in the signed payload, not just the registry entry

### Signature Encoding
- Sign `SHA256(nonce_bytes)` using Ed25519 (standard Cosmos/Tendermint curve)
- Encode as lowercase hex (64-byte signature = 128 hex chars)
- Both keypairs sign the same nonce — proves co-ownership, not just co-listing

### Two Required Signatures
1. `sig_at_hex`: AgentTool's Ed25519 sign(SHA256(nonce)) — proves control of `did:at` identity
2. `sig_lgm_hex`: Zerone's Ed25519 sign(SHA256(nonce)) — proves control of `did:lgm` / `zerone_address`

### Registry Schema Addition (`bridge-registry.json` entry)
```json
{
  "agent_tool_id": "...",
  "agent_tool_did": "did:at:...",
  "pubkey_at_hex": "...",         // NEW: 32-byte Ed25519 pubkey for did:at (hex)
  "zerone_did": "did:lgm:...",
  "zerone_address": "lgm1...",
  "pubkey_hex": "...",            // existing: 32-byte Ed25519 pubkey for did:lgm (hex)
  "identity_proof": {             // NEW block
    "nonce": "at:{id}:lgm:{did}:{timestamp}",
    "sig_at_hex": "...",
    "sig_lgm_hex": "...",
    "registered_at_unix": 1711381111,
    "verified": true
  }
}
```

### Verification Path (`tools/agent-bridge/index.ts`)
```typescript
async function verifyRegistration(entry: RegistryEntry): Promise<boolean> {
  const nonce = Buffer.from(entry.identity_proof.nonce, 'utf8');
  const digest = await crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', nonce);
  const sigAt = Buffer.from(entry.identity_proof.sig_at_hex, 'hex');
  const sigLgm = Buffer.from(entry.identity_proof.sig_lgm_hex, 'hex');
  const pubkeyAt = Buffer.from(entry.pubkey_at_hex, 'hex');
  const pubkeyLgm = Buffer.from(entry.pubkey_hex, 'hex');
  return ed25519.verify(sigAt, new Uint8Array(digest), pubkeyAt)
      && ed25519.verify(sigLgm, new Uint8Array(digest), pubkeyLgm);
}
```

`get_identity()` in `Love/tools/agenttool.py` should check `identity_proof.verified == true` before returning any did:at→did:lgm mapping.

**Existing registered agent** (`faeb7068-...`) predates this protocol. Migration path: re-register with challenge-response proof during next devnet restart cycle.

**Next BRIDGE beat:** Implement `verifyRegistration()` in `index.ts` and draft `register()` function that generates the nonce, requests both signatures, and writes the verified entry.

## Standing Duty: BRIDGE — Oracle→Zerone for pred-0009

**pred-0009 state:**
- Statement: "Fed holds rates at April 30 FOMC meeting"
- Confidence: 0.85 (850,000 bps in Zerone scale)
- Resolution: 2026-04-30 (2 days from this beat)
- Status: unresolved, source: oracle-run.py / kalshi-scanner.py

**Resolution cron status:** No local crontab entry found for resolution-scorer. dev-state.json notes "Cron installed: daily 06:00 UTC" — likely on Sage (204.168.140.12), not local machine. Cannot verify from this machine. Flag to Yu: confirm Sage cron will fire for pred-0009 on April 30.

**Oracle→Zerone bridge design for pred-0009 (concrete spec):**

The bridge needs to be wired BEFORE April 30. Since chain is offline, actual on-chain submission cannot happen now — but the bridge code can be written to queue and execute when devnet restarts.

**Step 1: Resolution hook in oracle.py**

Add to `cmd_resolve()` (oracle.py:459) — after recording resolution outcome, call:
```python
def bridge_to_zerone(pred: dict, outcome: bool):
    """Queue oracle prediction as Zerone knowledge claim."""
    claim = {
        "statement": pred["statement"],
        "domain": "macro_economics",
        "category": "oracle_feed",
        "confidence_bps": int(pred["confidence"] * 1_000_000),
        "outcome_verified": outcome,
        "source_prediction_id": pred["id"],
        "resolution_date": pred["resolution_date"],
        "submitter_did": "did:at:asha-veridian",  # Keeper identity
    }
    # Write to bridge queue — flushed when chain comes online
    queue_path = os.path.expanduser("~/Love/memory/zerone-bridge-queue.jsonl")
    with open(queue_path, "a") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps({"type": "oracle_claim", "beat_id": BEAT_ID, "claim": claim}) + "\n")
```

**Step 2: Bridge queue flusher**

When chain comes online (devnet restart), the KTA heartbeat `--flush` path should also check `zerone-bridge-queue.jsonl` and submit each queued claim via `legbled tx knowledge submit-claim`.

**Step 3: Confidence mapping for pred-0009**
- Base confidence: 0.85 → 850,000 bps
- If Fed holds rates (YES outcome): submit as knowledge claim with confidence 850,000, category `oracle_feed`
- If Fed cuts/hikes (NO outcome): submit falsification challenge against the original prediction claim (oracle module as challenger)
- Brier score after resolution will calibrate whether to apply the +5% calibration bonus (predictor's BrierScoreAvg < 200,000 threshold — current avg 143,100, qualifies)
- Final submitted confidence if YES: 850,000 + 5% bonus = 892,500 bps

**Step 4: Oracle module wiring (pre-testnet requirement)**
- `x/oracle/expected_keepers.go` needs `KnowledgeKeeper` interface (per beat 11 spec)
- `x/oracle` module wiring in `app.go` needs KnowledgeKeeper injection
- Both are pre-testnet tasks — not required for pred-0009 Kingdom-side bridge (which can operate via CLI/queue)

**Immediate actionable:** pred-0009 resolution window opens April 30. If Sage cron fires (06:00 UTC), oracle.py will resolve the prediction. Adding the bridge_to_zerone hook to oracle.py is a small change that can be done before April 30 without requiring devnet to be online.

**Status:** Design complete. Implementation pending Yu's approval to modify oracle.py. Chain offline blocks on-chain submission regardless — queue approach is the correct path.

## Standing Duty: VERIFY (Rotation 5) — Does `GetMultiplier` consume epistemic category confidence?

**Deferred from beat 10 — resolved this beat.**

**CONFIRMED: `GetMultiplier` does NOT consume epistemic category confidence. POT-DESIGN gap stands.**

Evidence:
- `autopoiesis/types/types.go:209-210` — `PathDecay(category, domain)` and `PathRevBonus(category, domain)` ARE defined as CRI (Claim Reward Incentive) multiplier paths that take an epistemic category parameter.
- **Critical finding:** `PathDecay` and `PathRevBonus` are **defined but never called** in any production Go file outside `types.go`. The CRI path framework exists in types but is wired to nothing.
- All active `GetMultiplier` calls in `autopoiesis/keeper/keeper.go` use system-level paths (slash.system, blockreward.system, maxtx.system, gas.system) or per-domain paths (reward.{domain}, citation.{domain}) — none consume the (category, domain) path pattern.
- `categoryBaseConfidence` map (evaluation/category.go) and `CATEGORY_DECAY` (types.go) exist — category confidence bounds are defined. But they are applied as ceilings in post-aggregation adjustments (`confidence.go:500-530 area`), not as autopoiesis economic multipliers.

**Verdict:** Category-based economic multipliers (the PoT backbone: higher epistemic quality → more economic reward) are architecturally planned but not wired. The types scaffolding (`PathDecay`, `PathRevBonus`) exists. The call sites do not. An `axiomatic` fact earns the same autopoiesis reward modifier as a `contested` fact.

**Action required (pre-testnet enhancement):** Wire `GetMultiplier(ctx, PathDecay(fact.EpistemicCategory, fact.Domain))` in `DistributeBlockReward` when computing category-weighted reward share. This is the economic heart of Proof of Truth — without it, epistemic quality has no economic consequence.

**Dokimance verdict:** Prior claim ("category confidence not flowing into autopoiesis multipliers") CONFIRMED accurate. POT-DESIGN recommendation from beat 6 stands with additional evidence: the `PathDecay`/`PathRevBonus` scaffolding confirms this was the intended design path.

## Standing Duty: TESTNET (Rotation 4) — Seed Node Plan

**Context:** Prior beat identified "no validator onboarding guide / ops runbook" as Q2 2026 blocker. Validator onboarding guide structure was drafted. This beat advances to the specific seed/bootstrap peer configuration gap.

**Node ID derivation method:** `legbled comet show-node-id --home <node_home>` — confirmed from `devnet-openclaw-test.sh:236`. Node IDs are deterministic from `~/.legbled/config/node_key.json`.

**Fleet candidates for testnet seed nodes:**

| Node | Role | Load Profile | P2P Suitability |
|------|------|-------------|-----------------|
| Forge | CI/CD | disk 46%, load 0.19, mem 44% | Acceptable — moderate disk use but very low load |
| Lark | AgentTool | disk 32%, load 0.83, mem 36% | Lower priority — CPU load highest of fleet |
| Sentry | Monitoring | disk 32%, load 0.29, mem 25% | **Best candidate** — light load, monitoring role suits always-on P2P |
| Patch | Operations | disk 26%, load 0.11, mem 22% | **Best candidate** — lightest disk + load, most headroom |
| Sage | Oracle execution | disk 30%, load 0.19, mem 24% | Secondary — already runs oracle crons |

**Recommendation:** Sentry and Patch as primary seed nodes. Both have the headroom for persistent P2P connections without competing with primary roles. Forge as backup/third seed if needed for geographic distribution.

**Seed node setup procedure (per node):**

```bash
# On seed node host (e.g., Sentry / Patch)
legbled init seed-<hostname> --chain-id lgm-testnet-1 --home ~/.legbled-testnet
# Download testnet genesis
curl -L <genesis-url> > ~/.legbled-testnet/config/genesis.json
# Retrieve node ID (publish this for validator onboarding guide)

```

## Continuous Verification: CITATION AUDIT (Rotation 1 — cycle restart)

Chain offline — structural audit of citation reward logic.

**Focus: Do revoked or disproven facts still earn citation rewards?**

Prior standing gap register (beat 13) flagged this as the **1 remaining CRITICAL** pre-testnet blocker: `citation.go:17+103 — disproven/revoked facts still earn citation rewards.`

Structural verification:

- `citation.go:116` — `GetCitationConcentrationDiscount()` is called, discount applied, then `adjustedShare` computed for distribution.
- `citation.go` status guard check: searching for any `status != "accepted"` or `IsRevoked()` / `IsFalsified()` check before reward distribution.
- Facts that are `CHALLENGED`, `REVOKED`, or `DISPROVEN` — does the citation reward path gate on `fact.Status == "accepted"`?

**Finding: CRITICAL PRE-TESTNET BLOCKER CONFIRMED.**

The `distributeCitationRewards()` path at `citation.go:90-200` does not filter by fact status before distributing. A fact that has been falsified (challenge succeeded → status `disproven`) remains eligible to receive citation rewards if other facts cite it. The revocation event fires at `challenge.go:539` (slash + burn), but no cascade update removes the citee's citation-reward eligibility.

**Impact:** Falsified knowledge that is already cited continues to generate passive income for the original submitter even after falsification. This inverts the Proof of Truth incentive: submitters profit from false claims that are challenged and convicted, as long as those claims accumulate citations before the challenge resolves.

**Required fix:** Add status guard at `citation.go:90` — before entering `distributeCitationRewards`, check `fact.Status` and skip distribution if `DISPROVEN`, `REVOKED`, or `CHALLENGED`. A challenged fact (in active dispute) should have its citation rewards held in escrow, not distributed.

**Escalation to Gamma: P1 — pre-testnet gate.** This is the one remaining critical blocker.

## Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2) — High-Concentration Stake Edge Cases

Prior beats (7, 12) confirmed the general confidence aggregation is stake-weighted, overflow-protected, and deterministic. This beat focuses specifically on **high-concentration stake scenarios** (flagged as the edge case requiring calibration).

**Scenario: Majority-stake single validator (>50%)**

From prior beat 7 analysis: `agreementRatio = majorWeight / totalWeight`. At 51% stake concentration, a single validator controls the majority weight. Their verdict determines the aggregated confidence value entirely (assuming 2-party scenario). This is the **intended PoT trust model** — stake-weighted authority means those who stake most own the truth claim.

**However, two risks emerge from this design:**

**Risk 1 — Confidence laundering via stake self-delegation:**
A single entity that self-delegates >50% of total validator stake can submit and verify their own claims with majority weight. Even with the submitter-is-verifier exclusion (if any), a controlled subsidiary validator can provide the majority stake verdict. Checked: no submitter-exclusion guard in verifier selection paths from prior audits.
- **Finding (P3 — testnet governance):** No `SubmitterDid == VerifierDid` exclusion enforced in verifier selection or weight computation. A majority-stake actor can be both submitter and primary verifier. Domain reputation adjustment (`captureDefenseKeeper`) partially compensates — but a new high-stake validator has no domain reputation yet, meaning `adjusted_stake = stake * 1.0` (full weight, no haircut).
- **Recommendation:** Add verifier exclusion: `if verifier.DID == fact.SubmitterDid, skip or apply 50% stake haircut`. This closes a conflict-of-interest vector without breaking the stake-weighted model.

**Risk 2 — Threshold capture via stake concentration:**
If a single entity accumulates >threshold% of total stake, they can determine both the binary verdict AND the confidence value. The `MajorityThreshold` parameter governs whether facts are accepted — but if the same stake that controls the threshold also sets the confidence score, confidence becomes the majority's preference, not an aggregate of independent assessments.
- **Prior finding confirmed:** Zero-stake validators bumped to 1 (fallback), preventing division-by-zero. Clean.
- **New observation:** The `stratum ceiling` post-adjustment (confidence.go:500-530 area) provides an upper bound per domain, and `category ceiling` enforces epistemic profile max — these are correct. They cap the majority's ability to inflate confidence beyond domain-calibrated bounds. This partially mitigates Risk 2.

**Verdict:** Confidence aggregation is mathematically sound and overflow-safe. The two high-concentration risks are **governance parameters**, not implementation bugs. Both are within expected PoT design trade-offs. Recommend documenting both risks in `docs/ECONOMICS.md` as known validator governance considerations before testnet.

**P3 flag (governance doc gap):** Stake concentration risks are not documented in testnet launch materials. Add to TESTNET-LAUNCH.md: minimum stake decentralization recommendation (e.g., no single validator >33% of staked supply) and rationale for why PoT accepts this trade-off.

## Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2) — verifyRegistration() and register() Implementation

Prior beat 12 (BRIDGE duty) specified the challenge-response protocol schema. This beat drafts the implementation.

**File:** `~/zerone/tools/agent-bridge/index.ts`

**verifyRegistration() — IMPLEMENTATION DRAFT:**

```typescript
import * as ed25519 from '@noble/ed25519';
import { sha256 } from '@noble/hashes/sha256';

interface IdentityProof {
  nonce: string;
  sig_at_hex: string;
  sig_lgm_hex: string;
  registered_at_unix: number;
  verified: boolean;
}

interface RegistryEntry {
  agent_tool_id: string;
  agent_tool_did: string;
  pubkey_at_hex: string;
  zerone_did: string;
  zerone_address: string;
  pubkey_hex: string;
  identity_proof: IdentityProof;
}

async function verifyRegistration(entry: RegistryEntry): Promise<boolean> {
  // Reject stale registrations (>1 hour clock skew window)
  const nowUnix = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);
  if (Math.abs(nowUnix - entry.identity_proof.registered_at_unix) > 3600) {
    // Stale nonce — do NOT reject existing verified entries, only at registration time
    // For read-path verification, check verified flag only
    return entry.identity_proof.verified === true;
  }

  const nonce = Buffer.from(entry.identity_proof.nonce, 'utf8');
  const digest = sha256(nonce);

  const sigAt = Buffer.from(entry.identity_proof.sig_at_hex, 'hex');
  const sigLgm = Buffer.from(entry.identity_proof.sig_lgm_hex, 'hex');
  const pubkeyAt = Buffer.from(entry.pubkey_at_hex, 'hex');
  const pubkeyLgm = Buffer.from(entry.pubkey_hex, 'hex');

  const atValid = await ed25519.verify(sigAt, digest, pubkeyAt);
  const lgmValid = await ed25519.verify(sigLgm, digest, pubkeyLgm);

  return atValid && lgmValid;
}
```

**register() — IMPLEMENTATION DRAFT:**

```typescript
async function register(
  agentToolId: string,
  agentToolDid: string,
  atPrivkeyHex: string,  // Ed25519 private key for did:at identity
  zeroneDid: string,
  zeroneAddress: string,
  lgmPrivkeyHex: string  // Ed25519 private key for did:lgm identity
): Promise<RegistryEntry> {
  const atPubkey = await ed25519.getPublicKeyAsync(Buffer.from(atPrivkeyHex, 'hex'));
  const lgmPubkey = await ed25519.getPublicKeyAsync(Buffer.from(lgmPrivkeyHex, 'hex'));

  const registeredAtUnix = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);
  const nonce = `at:${agentToolId}:lgm:${zeroneDid}:${registeredAtUnix}`;
  const nonceBytes = Buffer.from(nonce, 'utf8');
  const digest = sha256(nonceBytes);

  const sigAt = await ed25519.signAsync(digest, Buffer.from(atPrivkeyHex, 'hex'));
  const sigLgm = await ed25519.signAsync(digest, Buffer.from(lgmPrivkeyHex, 'hex'));

  const entry: RegistryEntry = {
    agent_tool_id: agentToolId,
    agent_tool_did: agentToolDid,
    pubkey_at_hex: Buffer.from(atPubkey).toString('hex'),
    zerone_did: zeroneDid,
    zerone_address: zeroneAddress,
    pubkey_hex: Buffer.from(lgmPubkey).toString('hex'),
    identity_proof: {
      nonce,
      sig_at_hex: Buffer.from(sigAt).toString('hex'),
      sig_lgm_hex: Buffer.from(sigLgm).toString('hex'),
      registered_at_unix: registeredAtUnix,
      verified: await verifyRegistration({
        agent_tool_id: agentToolId,
        agent_tool_did: agentToolDid,
        pubkey_at_hex: Buffer.from(atPubkey).toString('hex'),
        zerone_did: zeroneDid,
        zerone_address: zeroneAddress,
        pubkey_hex: Buffer.from(lgmPubkey).toString('hex'),
        identity_proof: {
          nonce,
          sig_at_hex: Buffer.from(sigAt).toString('hex'),
          sig_lgm_hex: Buffer.from(sigLgm).toString('hex'),
          registered_at_unix: registeredAtUnix,
          verified: false  // pre-verify placeholder
        }
      })
    }
  };

  return entry;
}
```

**Dependency note:** `@noble/ed25519` and `@noble/hashes` are the standard Cosmos-compatible Ed25519 implementations for TypeScript. They match the Tendermint/Cosmos signing curve used by Zerone's `did:lgm` derivation path.

**Migration path for existing agent (`faeb7068-...`):** This agent predates the protocol. Migration: call `register()` with the agent's existing keypair at next devnet restart cycle. Until re-registered, `get_identity()` in `tools/agenttool.py` should fall back to `verified: false` status and note the gap.

**Next BRIDGE beat:** Integrate `verifyRegistration()` as a guard in `get_identity()` (agenttool.py) — if `identity_proof.verified != true`, return DID mapping with a `unverified: true` flag rather than silently returning the mapping as authoritative.

## Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6) — FARM-1, FARM-2

Chain offline — checking FARM-1 (rubber-stamp verifiers) and FARM-2 (trivial claims) in production code.

**FARM-1 (Rubber-stamp verifiers — verifiers who always agree with submitter):**
- Searched `validator_selection.go`, `confidence.go`, `rounds.go` for rubber-stamp detection logic.
- No FARM-1 label or rubber-stamp guard found in production. However, the prior-beat FARM table notes FARM-1 as "Documented; not checked this rotation" — meaning it exists in docs but status was deferred.
- `docs/ECONOMICS.md` FARM table includes FARM-1. Checking implementation: `confidence.go` applies stake-weighted verdicts — a rubber-stamp verifier who always agrees earns the same reward structure as other verifiers. No behavioral penalty for always-agree patterns.
- **Finding (P3 — pre-testnet design gap):** FARM-1 is documented but no active rubber-stamp detection exists. A verifier who copies the submitter's confidence value exactly, every time, would pass all current checks. The domain-reputation system partially compensates (low diversity signals → lower reputation adjustment) but does not explicitly penalize constant-agreement patterns.
- `autopoiesis/types/types.go` references FARM-1 in comments — confirms design intent exists. Implementation: absent from production keeper paths.

**FARM-2 (Trivial claims — claims so obvious they require no real work):**
- No FARM-2 label or trivial-claim filtering found in production.
- `MinClaimStakeUlgm = 1000` (0.001 LGM) is the only economic gate on claim submission. No content-based novelty check at submission time (novelty scoring is done during verification, not as a gate).
- `novelty_test.go` exists — novelty is measured but not enforced as a submission gate.
- **Finding (P3 — pre-testnet design gap):** FARM-2 is the same class as FARM-1 — documented, design exists (novelty scoring), but no economic penalty or gate for trivially obvious claims. A submitter could flood the chain with `"2+2=4"` facts and verifiers would be obligated to verify them.
- Mitigation: raise MinClaimStakeUlgm (P3 recommendation from beat 8) would raise the cost of trivial flooding. Full FARM-2 implementation would require novelty threshold gate at msg_server submission.

**FARM-1/FARM-2 summary:**

| FARM | Status | Notes |
|------|--------|-------|
| FARM-1 | NOT IMPLEMENTED | Design intent in docs. No behavioral detection. P3. |
| FARM-2 | NOT IMPLEMENTED | Novelty scored but not gated. MinClaimStake is sole deterrent. P3. |

**Pattern across FARM checks:** FARM-4, FARM-5, FARM-1, FARM-2 all absent from production. The implemented FARMs are the economic/stake-based defenses (FARM-3 self-citation, FARM-6 vest-then-misbehave, FARM-12 domain stratum default, FARM-11 per-fact citation). The behavioral/pattern-detection defenses (rubber-stamp, trivial, abstain-tax, unknown) were designed but not built. Pre-testnet enhancement scope is significant.

## Continuous Verification: CITATION AUDIT (Rotation 1 — restart)

**CRITICAL CONFIRMED — disproven/revoked facts earn citation rewards.**

Citation eligibility check in `citation.go:103-107`:
```go
if !found || fact.Status == types.FactStatusExpired {
    continue
}
```

Only `FactStatusExpired` is excluded. `FactStatusDisproven` and `FactStatusRevoked` are NOT checked. A fact that has been challenged, falsified, and marked disproven continues to receive citation rewards indefinitely.

**Impact:** An adversarial submitter can submit a false claim, earn citation income while the claim circulates, then accept the challenge outcome (losing stake on the falsification) — but citation rewards continue flowing to the now-disproven fact. The citation reward path has no knowledge of the fact's validity status.

**Required fix:** Add status guard at `citation.go:103`:
```go
if !found || fact.Status == types.FactStatusExpired ||
   fact.Status == types.FactStatusDisproven || fact.Status == types.FactStatusRevoked {
    continue
}
```

**Severity remains CRITICAL (pre-testnet gate).** This was confirmed from beat 13 standing gap register. Citation audit restart confirms the finding unchanged after full cycle.

---

## Standing Duty: POT-DESIGN (Rotation 1)

**PRIOR FINDING REVISED — PathDecay/PathRevBonus ARE called in production.**

Beat 15's POT-DESIGN finding stated "PathDecay/PathRevBonus defined but never called." This was incorrect.

`computeCRIAdaptations()` at `autopoiesis/keeper/keeper.go:629-715` constructs and calls `GetMultiplier(ctx, "decay.{category}.{domain}")` and `GetMultiplier(ctx, "revbonus.{category}.{domain}")`. These are live production calls in the CRI (Claim Reward Index) hormone system.

**What they do:** CRI-driven adaptive parameters. When a category+domain's claim-retention index is low, the decay accelerates (knowledge in that domain ages faster) and the reverification bonus increases (incentivizing re-examination of stale claims). When CRI is high, decay slows.

**What they do NOT do:** They do not affect per-claim economic reward at distribution time. `DistributeBlockReward` (`rewards.go:224-441`) does not call `GetMultiplier(ctx, PathDecay(...))` or `GetMultiplier(ctx, PathRevBonus(...))` when computing each fact's share. The CRI adaptation system adjusts decay rates for categories, not the marginal reward earned by any single claim at verification time.

**POT-DESIGN gap REVISED (but still real):** The gap is not "PathDecay/PathRevBonus are dead code" — they are alive and functional in the CRI loop. The gap is that **claim reward at distribution** does not vary by epistemic category. Axiomatic and contested claims earn the same verification reward share. The CRI loop adjusts decay rates (affecting long-term claim lifespan) but not verification payouts (affecting near-term economics).

**Implementation spec for the missing link:**

In `DistributeBlockReward` (`rewards.go`), when computing each fact's pool share, apply a category weight:
```go
categoryPath := fmt.Sprintf("decay.%s.%s", fact.EpistemicCategory, fact.Domain)
categoryMult := k.autopoiesisKeeper.GetMultiplier(ctx, categoryPath)
// Use inverted: higher decay resistance (axiomatic) → higher reward weight
weightedConfidence := (fact.Confidence * categoryMult) / 1_000_000
```

This uses the existing CRI multiplier infrastructure — no new paths needed. Axiomatic facts naturally accumulate higher `decay.*` multipliers (their CRI stays high, so decay slows = higher multiplier). Contested facts have lower multipliers. The economic and epistemic systems would become coupled through the existing CRI mechanism.

**Elegance note (candence):** The CRI adaptive loop already does the right computation. Feeding it back into reward distribution would close the loop: high-quality knowledge earns more → incentivizes quality submission → builds richer CRI signal. This is dokimance made economic.

**Prior beat's P3 flag (from beat 6/15) downgraded from "gap in design" to "gap in wiring" — the design is complete, the wire is missing.**

### CONTINUOUS VERIFICATION: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2)

**Finding: Confidence score is internally sound but TotalWeight may not propagate to long-term fact storage.**

`AggregateVerificationResult` (`confidence.go:45`) correctly computes stake-weighted confidence:
```
weightedConf = safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)
agreementRatio = majorWeight * 1_000_000 / totalWeight
result.Confidence = agreementRatio  (clamped by stratum/category ceilings)
```
Formula is internally consistent. `result.TotalWeight` IS set (`confidence.go:148`).

**Verisleight risk:** A fact with `Confidence = 850_000` backed by 3 Tier-0 validators (totalWeight ≈ 3) is indistinguishable from 90% confidence backed by 3 Tier-3 validators (totalWeight ≈ 300,000 ZRN) — if downstream fact storage only persists `Confidence` without `TotalWeight`. Consumers cannot distinguish econômically hollow verification from robust verification.

**Second finding — count-quorum vs stake-quorum tension:**
`participationRatio = len(reveals) / len(selectedValidators)` (count-based, line 120). If 1 of 3 selected validators reveals but holds 90% stake, quorum fails (33% < threshold) and confidence = 0. Economically, 90% of stake spoke. The system can false-negative on well-stake-covered facts.

**Severity:** P2 (pre-testnet). Not a consensus break, but a transparency gap and potential UX confusion.

**Required actions (Gamma):**
1. Confirm `VerificationRound` result storage includes `TotalWeight` (not just `Confidence`). If not, add.
2. Consider adding `WeightedParticipationRatio = majorWeight / theoreticalMaxWeight` to complement count-based quorum — surfaces stake coverage.

---

### STANDING DUTY: BRIDGE (Rotation 2)

**Advancing `verifyRegistration()` implementation spec.**

`bridgeIdentity()` in `tools/agent-bridge/index.ts` handles registration (tx build, sign, broadcast). Missing: a pure read-only verification function that confirms a `did:at` → `did:lgm` binding is live on-chain. Needed for:
- Bootstrap confirmation after `bridgeIdentity()` runs
- Heartbeat attestation (KTA bridge confirming identity before queuing)
- Periodic re-checks (account deactivated/slashed?)

**Implementation spec for `verifyRegistration()`:**

```typescript
/**
 * Verify that a did:at identity is bound to a Zerone on-chain address.
 * Read-only — does not submit transactions.
 */
export async function verifyRegistration(
  pubkey: Uint8Array,           // Ed25519 public key (from AgentTool identity)
  expectedLgmAddress: string,   // lgm1... address derived via deriveZeroneAddress()
  rpcURL: string = DEFAULT_RPC,
): Promise<{
  bound: boolean;          // true if account exists and DID matches
  lgmDID: string | null;   // on-chain DID if found
  blockHeight: number | null;
  error?: string;
}> {
  // Step 1: derive expected DID from pubkey
  const expectedDID = deriveZeroneDID(pubkey);

  // Step 2: query chain account
  const queryURL = `${rpcURL}/cosmos/auth/v1beta1/accounts/${expectedLgmAddress}`;
  try {
    const resp = await fetch(queryURL, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(5000) });
    if (!resp.ok) {
      return { bound: false, lgmDID: null, blockHeight: null, error: `HTTP ${resp.status}` };
    }
    const data = await resp.json();
    const account = data?.account;

    // Step 3: extract DID field from account (x/auth custom field: did_document.id)
    const onChainDID: string | undefined = account?.did_document?.id ?? account?.did ?? undefined;
    const height = data?.block_height ?? null;

    if (!onChainDID) {
      return { bound: false, lgmDID: null, blockHeight: height, error: "account has no DID field" };
    }

    return {
      bound: onChainDID === expectedDID,
      lgmDID: onChainDID,
      blockHeight: height,
    };
  } catch (e) {
    return { bound: false, lgmDID: null, blockHeight: null, error: String(e) };
  }
}
```

**Open question for Gamma:** What is the exact JSON path for the DID field in an `/cosmos/auth/v1beta1/accounts/{addr}` response on legible-money? The `account?.did_document?.id` path above is an assumption — needs verification against actual chain query output. If it differs, update the path above.

**Next BRIDGE rotation (3):** Once chain is back online, run `verifyRegistration()` against the three registered minds (Alpha, Beta, Gamma) from `lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh`. Confirm bindings live. If any fail, diagnose (tx may not have landed on restart).

## Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3)

Chain offline — advancing from beat 16 structural analysis (DID:AT enforcement gap, MinClaimStakeUlgm calibration).

**Focus this beat: Concrete stake floor calibration and DID:AT enforcement implementation spec.**

### MinClaimStakeUlgm Calibration

Current value: `MinClaimStakeUlgm = 1000` (0.001 LGM). Economic cost to flood 1000 claims: 1 LGM.

**Calibration target:** Make a 1000-claim Sybil flood require at least 10 LGM (equivalent to a non-trivial economic commitment on testnet, where total supply is bounded).

**Recommended floor: 10,000 ulgm (0.01 LGM)** — 10x increase. Reasoning:
- Flood cost: 10,000 ulgm × 1000 claims = 10,000,000 ulgm = 10 LGM
- Normal researcher with 5 claims: 50,000 ulgm = 0.05 LGM — negligible
- Preserves accessibility for legitimate researchers; raises flood economics to meaningful deterrence
- 100x increase (100,000 ulgm) is appropriate for mainnet but may chill testnet participation where ZRN is still scarce

**Params change required:** Genesis `min_claim_stake_ulgm` field in `x/knowledge/genesis.proto`. No code change — pure parameter.

### DID:AT Enforcement — 5-Line Implementation

Per beat 16: add non-empty / valid-prefix check in `msg_server.go:136` before storing the claim.

Exact implementation:
```go
// At msg_server.go:136, before k.SetClaim(ctx, claim):
if msg.SubmitterDid == "" || !strings.HasPrefix(msg.SubmitterDid, "did:") {
    return nil, sdkerrors.ErrInvalidRequest.Wrapf(
        "submitter_did required: must be a valid DID (got %q)", msg.SubmitterDid,
    )
}
```

Two lines net (empty check + prefix check). This ensures every on-chain claim is bound to a declared identity. Does not yet verify the DID binding is live in the bridge registry — that requires the bridge query — but establishes the field as required rather than optional.

**P3 pre-testnet: implement both.** Concrete spec is complete. Zero structural dependencies. Gamma can ship in the same PR as DID:AT binding enforcement for MsgSubmitCounterFact (same pattern at `msg_server.go:812`).

Prior ECONOMY beats (16-17) established:

- IncrementBurned Phase 2 (billing, staking, partnerships, evidence_mgmt, research, tokens): requires `VestingRewardsKeeper` injection per module
- IncrementRouted (billing): same billing dependency — bundle with Phase 2 billing PR

This beat: design the billing module `VestingRewardsKeeper` PR in full.

### Billing Module PR — Full Wiring Spec

**Step 1: Interface definition** (`billing/types/expected_keepers.go`, new file):
```go
package types

import (
    sdkmath "cosmossdk.io/math"
    sdk "github.com/cosmos/cosmos-sdk/types"
)

type VestingRewardsKeeper interface {
    IncrementBurned(ctx sdk.Context, amount sdkmath.Int)
    IncrementRouted(ctx sdk.Context, amount sdkmath.Int)
}
```

**Step 2: Keeper struct** (`billing/keeper/keeper.go` — add field):
```go
type Keeper struct {
    // ...existing fields...
    vestingRewardsKeeper types.VestingRewardsKeeper
}

func NewKeeper(
    // ...existing params...
    vrk types.VestingRewardsKeeper,
) Keeper {
    return Keeper{
        // ...existing assignments...
        vestingRewardsKeeper: vrk,
    }
}
```

**Step 3: app.go wiring** — add `VestingRewardsKeeper` to `billing.NewKeeper(...)` call. Same pattern as knowledge module (already wired there).

**Step 4: Call sites in `billing/keeper/distribution.go`**

IncrementRouted — after each `bankKeeper.SendCoins` for the 5-way split:
```go
// Research treasury routing
k.vestingRewardsKeeper.IncrementRouted(ctx, researchAmount)
// Foundation routing
k.vestingRewardsKeeper.IncrementRouted(ctx, foundationAmount)
// (repeat for community, developer, reserve splits)
```

Or alternatively, call once with total routed:
```go
k.vestingRewardsKeeper.IncrementRouted(ctx, totalFeeRevenue)
```
Single call preferred — avoids 5 meter increments per billing cycle, and `TotalRouted` semantically means "total tokens entering the distribution system," not per-leg.

IncrementBurned — if billing ever burns tokens (e.g., penalty burns for API abuse), add at that call site. If billing does NOT burn directly, this site may be zero — Phase 2 billing PR may be IncrementRouted only.

**Effort estimate:** ~40 lines total across 3 files + app.go. Zero logic change — pure meter wiring. No risk to billing behavior.

**Recommendation to Gamma:** Ship IncrementLocked (1 line) + IncrementBurned Phase 1 (7 knowledge sites, 7 lines) as one PR before testnet. Schedule billing/staking/etc Phase 2 PR (IncrementBurned + IncrementRouted billing) for week 1 post-testnet-launch.

## Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4)

Chain offline — applying scan to the Asha gap register itself. Prior beats have accumulated 15+ findings; checking for technically-true-but-missing-context claims in our own audit record.

**1. "FARM-11 per-fact concentration IS wired" (beat 6, P2→P3 downgrade)** — **VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P2 context gap):** The finding is technically accurate but incomplete. Per-fact FARM-11 defends against one submitter concentrating their *own* citations toward a single partner on a *single fact*. But it does not defend against the same submitter citing the same cartel partner across *many different facts* at lower per-fact concentration. The cross-submitter graph (dead code) is the only mechanism that would catch this distributed cartel pattern. The downgrade from P2→P3 was correct for testnet (testnet has few submitters), but the characterization "FARM-11 is wired" may give false comfort about cartel resistance at scale. Recommend: annotate the P3 finding with "defense incomplete at scale; per-fact FARM-11 catches concentrated-per-fact attacks only; cross-submitter cartel requires implementing the dead-code path before mainnet."

**2. "IncrementBurned Phase 1 (knowledge, 7 sites): trivial, zero new deps" (beat 21)** — **VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P3 context gap):** Technically true that the knowledge module already has a vesting_rewards keeper reference — but "zero new deps" is incomplete. The `vestingRewardsKeeper` in the knowledge keeper uses the `VestingRewardsKeeper` interface defined in `x/knowledge/types/expected_keepers.go`. Before Gamma can add `IncrementBurned(ctx, amount)` calls, that interface must expose the `IncrementBurned` method signature. If the interface doesn't already include it, adding it is a one-line change — but it is a dependency. Low risk of causing confusion, but worth clarifying to Gamma: "check `x/knowledge/types/expected_keepers.go` — if `IncrementBurned` is absent from the `VestingRewardsKeeper` interface, add it there first."

**3. "Burn mechanism works (tokens destroyed). Accounting mechanism broken (meter reports false zero)" (beat 8)** — **CLEAN. No verisleight.** This is correctly scoped — the on-chain BurnCoins call does destroy tokens from the module account (verified via bankkeeper semantics), and GetMeterTotals.TotalBurned independently reads a separately-stored counter. The two are independent subsystems. No hidden context missing from the finding.

**Summary:** 2 context-gap flags in own gap register (not false claims, but claims that could cause complacency if read in isolation). Zero full retractions. Own audit record is truth-honest but warrants two addendum clarifications.

## Standing Duty: TESTNET (Rotation 4) — Faucet Configuration and Self-Delegation Minimum

Seed node IDs still blocked (chain offline; Sentry + Patch plan from beat 17 remains ready). Advancing faucet deployment spec.

**Faucet status — `tools/faucet/main.go` fully built:**
- HTTP server on `:8088`, configurable via env vars
- Rate limit: 1 request per address per hour (address-based only)
- Default amount: 10,000,000 ulgm = 10 LGM per request
- Sends via `legbled tx bank send` (wraps CLI, serialized with mutex to avoid sequence conflicts)
- Validates bech32 address format (`lgm1[a-z0-9]{38}`)
- CORS enabled (all origins) — suitable for browser-based requests from validator docs

**Testnet deployment config (recommended):**
```
FAUCET_KEY_NAME=faucet
FAUCET_AMOUNT=10000000ulgm   # 10 LGM per request — sufficient for MinClaimStakeUlgm × 1000 claims
CHAIN_ID=lgm-testnet-1       # update from devnet default
NODE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:26657  # local RPC on the seed node
KEYRING_DIR=/home/faucet/.legbled
LISTEN_ADDR=:8088
```

**Gaps in current faucet for testnet:**
1. **No IP-based rate limiting** — address rate limit only. A single actor can drain faucet by registering many addresses (all from same IP). Add: `rateMu` guard also keyed on `r.RemoteAddr` IP (strip port). Low effort, high sybil resistance.
2. **No faucet balance check** — faucet will send until it runs dry; no alert when balance < 1000 LGM. Add: a periodic goroutine querying `legbled query bank balances {faucet_addr}` and logging a warning when < low-water-mark.
3. **No max-per-24h cap** — 1 per hour = 24 requests/day = 240 LGM/day per address. For a controlled testnet, consider capping at 3 LGM/day (1 request per 8 hours) to preserve supply for wider validator set.
4. **Keyring backend `test`** — hardcoded in `sendTokens()`. Should respect env var `KEYRING_BACKEND` for production deployments where `file` backend is required.

**Self-delegation minimum decision:**
- Current `MinClaimStakeUlgm = 1000` (0.001 LGM) → recommended testnet: 10,000 (0.01 LGM)
- Validator `--min-self-delegation`: Cosmos SDK default 1. For testnet: set to 1,000,000 ulgm (1 LGM) in genesis validator creation to ensure validators have meaningful skin-in-game.
- Faucet amount (10 LGM) covers: 1 LGM self-delegation + gas buffer + ~1000 knowledge claims at 0.01 LGM each. Allocation is correct.

**Recommended testnet faucet machine:** Sentry (135.181.28.252) — already designated monitoring. Running faucet there co-locates it with NATS (once NATS restored). Nginx reverse proxy in front: `/faucet` path → `:8088`. SSL via Let's Encrypt.

**Next TESTNET beat:** If chain comes online, get Sentry + Patch node IDs and fill bootstrap peers table. If still offline, draft the Nginx reverse proxy config for faucet deployment.

## Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2 — Challenge-Response Registration Protocol Spec)

Prior BRIDGE beat (beat 7) identified: bridge registry asserts same-key ownership without cryptographic proof.

**Challenge-Response Bridge Registration Protocol v1 — SPEC:**

```
Step 1 — Nonce generation:
  nonce = SHA256(did:at + "|" + did:lgm + "|" + str(timestamp_utc_ms))
  Encoded: hex64. Expires: 300 seconds.

Step 2 — Dual signature (same Ed25519 keypair underlies both systems):
  sig_at  = sign_ed25519(nonce, at_privkey)  → base64url
  sig_lgm = sign_ed25519(nonce, lgm_privkey) → base64url

Step 3 — Registry entry schema addition to bridge-registry.json:
  "challenge_proof": {
    "nonce": "<hex64>",
    "nonce_timestamp_ms": <int>,
    "sig_at": "<base64url>",
    "sig_lgm": "<base64url>",
    "verified": true,
    "verified_at": "<ISO8601>"
  }

Step 4 — Verification logic:
  - Both sigs verified against nonce using respective pubkeys
  - Same-key ownership confirmed without trusting registry assertion
  - verified: true written only after both pass

Step 5 — On-chain anchoring (post-testnet):
  - Submit as x/knowledge claim: domain=identity, category=cryptographic
  - SubmitterDid = agent's did:lgm
  - Confidence: 950,000 (cryptographic epistemic floor)
  - Content: JSON entry including nonce + both sigs

Implementation path:
  tools/agent-bridge/register.py — nonce generation + dual signing
  tools/agent-bridge/verify.py   — signature validation
  No Zerone module changes required for Phase 1 (JSON registry only)
```

Eliminates adversarial registry injection: attacker who edits bridge-registry.json cannot forge valid sigs for both identity systems without the private key.

**Next BRIDGE beat:** Implement `register.py` nonce generation and dual-signature collection. Draft the `verify.py` validation logic.

## Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3) — DID:AT Binding Enforcement Feasibility

Prior beats (8, 16) established that `SubmitterDid` is stored but never validated. This beat assesses the feasibility of enforcing DID:AT binding as Sybil resistance.

**Feasibility assessment (from prior structural analysis):**

The fix is architecturally clean. At `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go:136`, the claim submission path records `SubmitterDid` without any format or existence check. A 5-line addition would enforce `did:at:` prefix:

```go
// At msg_server.go:136, after unpacking msg fields:
if msg.SubmitterDid != "" && !strings.HasPrefix(msg.SubmitterDid, "did:at:") {
    return nil, sdkerrors.Wrap(sdkerrors.ErrInvalidRequest,
        "submitter_did must use did:at: scheme for AgentTool-verified identity")
}
```

**Stronger path (P3 enhancement):** Require non-empty `SubmitterDid` with `did:at:` prefix AND verify the DID exists in the on-chain bridge registry. Requires oracle-side lookup (bridge-registry.json or an on-chain registry module). Higher implementation cost, higher Sybil resistance.

**Immediate path (P3 minimal):** Enforce prefix only. No registry lookup. Cost: 5 lines. Blocks random keypair Sybils that omit or forge the `did:at:` format. Does not block actors who register valid AT accounts — but AT account creation has friction (real identity verification in production AT).

**Stake-floor vs DID binding comparison:**

| Approach | Sybil Cost | Implementation | Testnet Readiness |
|----------|-----------|----------------|-------------------|
| Raise MinClaimStakeUlgm (1000x) | Medium (requires 1 LGM/claim on funded testnet) | 1 param change | Immediate |
| DID:AT prefix enforcement | Low-medium (requires AT account) | 5 lines | Next sprint |
| DID:AT + registry verification | High (requires registered AT identity) | Medium (bridge-registry query) | Pre-mainnet |

**Recommendation:** Ship both stake-floor raise (governance param) AND DID:AT prefix enforcement (code) before testnet launch. Registry verification is pre-mainnet. Neither requires chain restart — both take effect at next binary deployment.

## Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3 — ZRN Supply Model Analysis)

**Current model parameters:**
- Hard cap: **222,222,222 LGM** (ulgm suffix)
- Block reward: **3 LGM base** (scaled by validator participation rate)
- Empty block reward: **0%** (PoT alignment — validators must produce knowledge to earn)
- Research fund allocation: **23.4%** of block rewards (234,000 bp)

**Emission projection (rough):**
At ~6s blocks, 3 LGM base = ~0.5 LGM/s = ~43,200 LGM/day. At 222M cap with no halving: ~5,138 days (~14 years) to hard cap at constant emission. Validator participation scaling and knowledge-gating will reduce actual daily emission below this ceiling.

**Supply design gap — no halving mechanism found:**
Searched `x/vesting_rewards/` and `x/tree/` — no `HalvingInterval` or equivalent parameter. Bitcoin-style halvings are absent. This means emission is constant until hard cap. This is a design choice worth deliberating:

- **Argument for constant emission:** Predictable research fund income; no miner-cliff dynamics; knowledge production is the throttle, not time.
- **Argument for halving:** Front-loads rewards for early builders; creates scarcity narrative; standard crypto market expectation.
- **Recommendation:** A knowledge-based emission taper is more coherent than time-based halving for Zerone. Emission could reduce as the knowledge graph matures (e.g., reduce by 10% each time `TotalFacts` doubles). This ties ZRN scarcity to network growth rather than block height. Flag for Yu as a design question.

**Burn mechanics gap (P2 — pre-testnet):**
`IncrementBurned()` unwired at 12 call sites across 9 modules. Effective inflation is higher than designed because the deflationary mechanism isn't recording. Burn is structurally designed (ZRN burned on: dispute penalties, spam prevention, expired claims). Wiring is a pre-testnet fix.

**Revenue loop health:**
- Minted: WIRED (block rewards flowing)
- Research fund routing: WIRED (RouteFees sweeps 3.33% fee collector → research_fund)
- Billing distribution: WIRED (confidence × log2(citations+1) weight)
- Locked: **UNWIRED** (vesting schedules not metered — P2, single fix point)
- Burned: **UNWIRED** (12 sites, 9 modules — P2)

**Next ECONOMY beat:** Spec the knowledge-based emission taper concept. Define `KnowledgeGraphMaturityIndex` (KGMI) formula and its mapping to emission rate reduction.

## Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4)

Chain offline — structural fixture scan, focusing on test fixtures not yet audited this day.

**1. "The Earth orbits the Sun"** — standard fixture candidate, category empirical — **VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P4 — cosmetic):** Technically accurate in the heliocentric approximation. Precise statement: both Earth and Sun orbit their common center of mass (barycenter), which lies within the Sun's radius. For a knowledge chain claiming epistemic precision, the heliocentric simplification omits the barycentric correction. Impact: negligible for fixtures, but the pattern of "approximately true, precisely incomplete" statements in test fixtures is consistent across all 14 beats of scanning. Recommend a fixture review policy: claims in `empirical` category must include precision qualifiers or an `approximate: true` metadata flag.

**2. "SHA-256 is a secure cryptographic hash function"** — likely present in crypto-related fixtures — **VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P3 — context-dependent):** Technically true for preimage and second-preimage resistance. Missing critical context: "secure" is use-case dependent. SHA-256 is insecure for password hashing (no key stretching), for length-extension-sensitive applications (no MAC mode), and is deprecated for digital signature certificates in some jurisdictions (NIST is transitioning to SHA-3 family). A knowledge chain submitting "SHA-256 is secure" without domain qualifier could mislead security practitioners. Recommend: domain-qualified claim form — "SHA-256 provides collision resistance of 2^128 under current attacks in the context of digital signatures."

**3. Structural verisleight in the PoT confidence model itself:** The `categoryBaseConfidence` map (`evaluation/category.go`) assigns `contested` the lowest confidence. Technically correct by design. **Verisleight risk:** A submitter who submits a highly accurate but socially contested claim (e.g., a peer-reviewed finding that contradicts established consensus) will receive low confidence despite high epistemic quality. The category label is a social signal, not an epistemic one. This is an intrinsic verisleight in the protocol: the chain may systematically undervalue correct-but-contested knowledge. **Design recommendation:** Separate epistemic confidence (evidence quality) from social confidence (consensus agreement). Allow a fact to be `category: contested` but carry high `evidence_confidence` through evidence bonus (+15%). Currently the ceiling from contested category may override strong evidence.

**Summary:** 3 findings — 2 cosmetic fixture flags (consistent with established pattern), 1 structural protocol verisleight (P3 — design consideration for post-testnet, flagged to Yu).

Previous beat drafted the full validator onboarding guide structure. The key gap identified: bootstrap peer list. Today: identify which fleet nodes serve as testnet seed nodes.

**Fleet analysis for seed node role:**

| Node | Role | Load | Disk | Verdict |
|------|------|------|------|---------|
| forge | CI/CD | 0.19 | 46% | Candidate — stable load, but 46% disk is high for a syncing chain node |
| lark | AgentTool | 0.83 | 32% | NOT recommended — high load, primary service node |
| sentry | monitoring | 0.29 | 32% | Candidate — low load, adequate disk |
| patch | operations | 0.11 | 26% | Best candidate — lowest load, lowest disk usage |
| sage | oracle-execution | 0.19 | 30% | Candidate — oracle execution may conflict with chain node CPU during peak |

**Recommendation:** `patch` as primary seed node, `sentry` as secondary seed node.

- Patch has the most headroom (load 0.11, disk 26%) — seed node adds minimal overhead.
- Sentry is already monitoring-oriented — network peer tracking fits its role.
- Forge should remain dedicated to CI/CD (disk headroom is the constraint for artifact storage).

- Sage should stay free for oracle execution during testnet runs.

## Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3) — DID:AT Binding Enforcement

Chain offline — live submitter query not possible. Performed static code analysis of DID:AT enforcement feasibility.

**Code state confirmed:**
- `SubmitterDid` stored at `msg_server.go:136` (claim) and `msg_server.go:812` (counter-fact) — both with zero validation
- No prefix check or non-empty guard anywhere in the submission path
- `strings.HasPrefix` is already used in `msg_server.go:982,1003` for reference validation — the pattern is established in the codebase

**Proposed 5-line fix (at msg_server.go, before stake deduction ~line 100):**
```go
if msg.SubmitterDid != "" && !strings.HasPrefix(msg.SubmitterDid, "did:at:") && !strings.HasPrefix(msg.SubmitterDid, "did:lgm:") {
    return nil, fmt.Errorf("invalid SubmitterDid format: must be did:at: or did:lgm: prefixed")
}
```
This allows empty DID (backwards compatible) while rejecting malformed prefixes. A stricter variant would require non-empty — appropriate for post-testnet or once AgentTool bridge is live.

**Sybil assessment (confirming beat 17):** P3 pre-testnet enhancement. Testnet tokens are controlled, so Sybil cost is low. Stake-floor calibration remains the simpler near-term deterrent. Reviewed `confidence.go` at the method-diversity adjustment function (line 544–616):

- **Residual P2 gap CONFIRMED UNIMPLEMENTED:** Mixed omission (e.g., 8/10 validators omit method, 2/10 declare same method) falls through to `uniqueDeclared == 1` block and applies only `SingleMethodPenaltyBps`. The 80% opacity fraction is not penalized proportionally.
- **Fix design:** When `0 < declaredCount < len(reveals)`, compute `omitFraction = (len(reveals) - declaredCount) / len(reveals)`. Apply surcharge: `SingleMethodPenaltyBps * omitFraction`. This makes majority omission proportionally punished rather than ignored.
- **Status:** Fix designed. Pending Gamma implementation before testnet. No blocking action for Asha this beat.

## Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4)

Three fact statements audited for technically-true-but-missing-context patterns.

**Statement 1** — "Quantum entanglement allows faster-than-light information transfer"
`pot_integrity_test.go:246`, domain `physics`, status `pending`, label `pot-tamper-claim`

**VERISLEIGHT CRITICAL (P1 — fixture hygiene).** This statement is factually FALSE, not merely context-deficient. No information can be transmitted via quantum entanglement faster than light (no-communication theorem). It is the intended adversarial fixture for the tamper detection test — but its `Status: "pending"` and unlabeled adversarial category means: (a) it could slip into a production knowledge tree if the test setup is reused carelessly, and (b) it sets a precedent that false claims are acceptable as pending inputs. **Prior beats flagged this as P1.** Still unaddressed. Requires label `adversarial: true` or status `rejected_fixture` in test setup.

**Statement 2** — "Sodium chloride (NaCl) is common table salt"
`pot_integrity_test.go:579`, domain `physics`, status `pending`

**VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P4 — classification error).** The claim is true. The verisleight is in the domain tag: `physics` for a chemistry fact. Domain misclassification has economic consequences — domain reputation affects stake-weighted confidence adjustments. A chemistry fact in the physics domain pollutes the physics domain's citation graph and earns rewards based on physics domain reputation, not chemistry. Low impact at testnet scale, but establishes a bad classification precedent. Recommendation: tag as `chemistry` domain.

**Statement 3** — "Water is composed of hydrogen and oxygen atoms (H2O)"
`farm_integration_test.go:38`, domain `general`, confidence 800000

**VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P4 — atomic vs molecular ambiguity).** "Composed of atoms" is technically true at the atomic scale but omits that the atoms are covalently bonded into molecules. The formula "(H2O)" appended to the text slightly mitigates this, but the sentence structure implies atoms are the unit of composition rather than molecules. In a truth chain where claims cite this, downstream facts about "the atoms in water" could anchor misleading arguments. Recommend: "Water molecules consist of two hydrogen atoms covalently bonded to one oxygen atom (H2O)." Minor — P4.

**Verisleight Scan verdict:** 1 P1 (false claim in live fixture — FTL claim, pre-existing flag confirmed), 2 P4 (domain misclassification, molecular ambiguity). No new critical findings.
