## Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK — FARM-3 Self-Citation Rings

**Verdict: IMPLEMENTED AND WIRED. No gap.**

Audited `citation.go:115-116`. FARM-3 defense (self-citation discount) is applied inline inside `DistributeCitationRewards`:

```go
if citingSubmitter != "" && fact.Submitter == citingSubmitter {
    adjustedShare = (adjustedShare * (1_000_000 - ep.SelfCitationDiscountBps)) / 1_000_000
}
```

This fires on every citation reward distribution where the citing submitter matches the cited fact's submitter. Default `SelfCitationDiscountBps` = 500,000 (50%). Self-citation rings cannot silently accumulate rewards — each self-cite earns only 50% of what a legitimate cross-submitter citation would earn.

**Clarification on prior P2 flag:** The P2 "citation cartel graph dead code" flag (beats 21-22) is FARM-11 (cross-submitter cartel detection via `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph` in `citation_defense.go`) — distinct from FARM-3. FARM-3 is clean; FARM-11 is still unwired.

**Additional FARM-11 note:** A separate per-fact concentration system IS wired via `GetCitationConcentrationDiscount` at `citation.go:275-296`, called from line 122. This provides a per-fact-level concentration defense using `CitationSourceRecord`. The unwired `citation_defense.go` functions operate at the per-submitter cross-network level — a different (deeper) cartel detection layer. Both layers are needed for full FARM-11 coverage.

**Updated flag status:** P2 FARM-11 → split into:
- P2-A: Per-submitter cartel graph (`UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph`) — unwired (escalation target: Gamma)
- P2-B: Per-fact concentration (`GetCitationConcentrationDiscount`) — WIRED (no action needed)

## Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6) — FARM-3 (self-citation rings)

FARM-3 queued since beat 5. Checked self-citation ring defense in `x/knowledge/keeper/citation.go`.

**Status: IMPLEMENTED AND WIRED.**

- `citation.go:112-116` — during citation reward distribution, checks if citing fact's submitter == cited fact's submitter.
- If self-citation detected: applies `SelfCitationDiscountBps` (50%) discount to the citing share.
- Production path: `citation.go` reward distribution loop, not test-only.

**Finding:** FARM-3 is defended at the reward level. A submitter who cites their own facts receives only 50% of the normal citation reward share. This disincentivizes self-citation rings economically.

**Residual gap (P4 — post-launch enhancement):** The 50% discount does not eliminate self-citation rings entirely — if the ring is profitable at half-rate, it still forms. A complete defense would also apply the per-fact concentration discount from FARM-11 on top of the self-citation discount. Currently these are independent checks. Together they would compound the penalty. No action required pre-testnet.

**Meter integrity update (follow-up from beat 10 flag):**
Beat 10 asked whether `IncrementLocked` and `IncrementRouted` are also dead code.
- `IncrementRouted`: **WIRED** — `vesting_rewards/keeper/rewards.go:122, 338`. Functional.
- `IncrementLocked`: **DEAD CODE** — definition only at `meter.go:67`. Zero production callers. `TotalLocked` returns stale/zero state.
- **P3 gap:** Locked token accounting is incorrect. Vesting schedules lock tokens, but `TotalLocked` meter never increments. Computable from vesting schedule iteration but the meter is the fast-path for governance queries. Add `IncrementLocked` call when vesting schedules are created.
- **Meter verdict:** Burned (P2) and Locked (P3) are decorative. Routed (P2.5) is partially wired. The meter subsystem provides false supply confidence to governance unless all three are wired.

## Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6 — Oracle→Knowledge bridge: implementation path)

Beat 4 designed three integration paths. This beat advances path 1 to concrete implementation.

**Oracle → Knowledge Bridge — Implementation Path**

Target: `MsgSubmitOraclePredictionAsClaim` — a new message type that wraps an oracle prediction as a knowledge claim.

**Wiring points:**

1. **New message type** — `x/knowledge/proto/knowledge/tx.proto`: add `MsgSubmitOraclePredictionAsClaim` with fields:
   - `submitter_did` (string) — agent DID making the claim
   - `oracle_prediction_id` (string) — Kalshi market ID
   - `probability_bps` (uint64) — 0–1,000,000 maps to 0–100% confidence
   - `domain` (string) — "oracle_prediction"
   - `resolution_deadline` (int64) — Unix timestamp of Kalshi resolution
   - `stake_amount` (sdk.Coin)

2. **Cross-module keeper** — `x/knowledge/keeper/keeper.go`: add `oracleKeeper` field. Interface: `OracleKeeper { GetPrediction(ctx, id) (Prediction, bool) }`. This is the same pattern already used by `captureDefenseKeeper` — low friction to add.

3. **Handler** — `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go`: new handler validates:
   - oracle prediction exists (via oracleKeeper.GetPrediction)
   - probability_bps → confidence (direct mapping: oracle probability = epistemic confidence)
   - submitter_did registered in bridge-registry (bridge keeper lookup)
   - Creates knowledge claim with `SubmitterDid` = `did:oracle:{market_id}`, category = "oracle_feed"

4. **Automated resolution** — `x/oracle/keeper/oracle_resolution_hook.go` (new file): when Kalshi resolution arrives, emit `oracle_resolved` event. Knowledge BeginBlocker subscribes: if `oracle_resolved` event and matching claim exists, call `AcceptFact()` (resolution = YES) or `ChallengeFact()` (resolution = NO).

5. **Revenue attribution** — no new code required. Existing billing knowledge pool (`confidence * log2(citations)`) automatically applies to oracle-origin claims once they are on-chain. Oracle submitter earns API revenue share proportional to prediction accuracy.

**Blocker:** Cross-system signature proof gap (identified beat 7, P2) — bridge-registry JSON trust assumption. Must resolve before oracle claims are weighted with the full DID-verified submitter bonus.

## Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3)

**ZRN Emission Ceiling Analysis — for Testnet Whitepaper**

Read `vesting_rewards/types/genesis.go`. Full economic parameter map:

| Parameter | Value | Meaning |
|-----------|-------|---------|
| BlockRewardAmount | 3,000,000 ulgm | 3 LGM base per block |
| FloorReward | 100,000 ulgm | 0.1 LGM minimum |
| MinValidatorsForFullReward | 22 | Target validator set |
| EmptyBlockRewardRate | 0 | No reward for empty blocks |
| ResearchShareBps | 33,300 | 3.33% of fees → research_fund |
| FounderShareBps | 70,000 | 7% of research fund |
| ReleasedClawbackRate | 3,300 | 33% returned on falsification |
| BlocksPerEpoch | 240,000 | ~1 week at 2521ms/block |
| DecayFactorBps | 850,000 | 0.85 decay per epoch |
| ApiSuppressionExponent | 2 | Quadratic (deferred) |

**Emission ceiling calculation:**

Block rewards follow geometric decay: weekly reward = 3 LGM × 240,000 blocks = 720,000 LGM (week 1), multiplied by 0.85 each subsequent week.

Total asymptotic emission = a / (1 - r) = 720,000 / (1 - 0.85) = **4,800,000 LGM**

At 52 weeks: 0.85^52 ≈ 0.000212 → cumulative year-1 emission ≈ 4,799,000 LGM (99.99% of ceiling). The decay mechanism is a practical hard cap within year 1.

**Economic gap identified (P3 — whitepaper/governance):** No explicit `MaxSupply` parameter exists in `Params`. The emission ceiling (4.8M LGM from block rewards) is implicit in the decay math, not stated on-chain. This means:
1. Governance cannot query the theoretical cap via a module parameter
2. External observers cannot verify the supply ceiling without computing the geometric series
3. If `DecayFactorBps` is governance-upgraded upward (e.g., 0.90), the ceiling rises to 7.2M LGM — not obviously flagged as a supply-expanding governance action

**Recommendation (P3 — pre-testnet governance docs):** Add a derived `ComputedSupplyCeiling()` view query to the module that returns `BlockRewardAmount × BlocksPerEpoch / (1 - DecayFactorBps/1_000_000)`. This makes the emission ceiling transparent and auditable without changing the economic logic.

Also confirmed: `ApiSuppressionExponent: 2` (quadratic billing suppression) is deferred. When activated, high-frequency API consumers will face quadratic cost scaling. This has no current economic effect but will materially change research fund inflow projections when activated.

## Standing Duty: POT-DESIGN (Rotation 1) — Multiplier Path Spec

Drafting the epistemic category → autopoiesis multiplier integration spec (prior beat recommendation).

**Integration point:** `vesting_rewards/keeper/rewards.go` — `DistributeBlockReward()` (lines 224-441).

**Spec:**

In `DistributeBlockReward`, when computing a verifier's reward share for a knowledge fact, insert a category multiplier lookup:

```go
// After computing baseShare for a verifier:
categoryPath := "knowledge.category." + fact.EpistemicCategory
categoryMul := k.autopoiesisKeeper.GetMultiplier(ctx, categoryPath) // returns BPS, 1_000_000 = 1.0x
adjustedShare := sdkmath.NewInt(baseShare).Mul(sdkmath.NewInt(int64(categoryMul))).Quo(sdkmath.NewInt(1_000_000))
```

**Genesis seeding for autopoiesis multiplier paths:**

| Category | Path | Multiplier BPS | Ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| axiomatic | knowledge.category.axiomatic | 1,200,000 | 1.20x |
| formal_proof | knowledge.category.formal_proof | 1,150,000 | 1.15x |
| on_chain | knowledge.category.on_chain | 1,100,000 | 1.10x |
| cryptographic | knowledge.category.cryptographic | 1,080,000 | 1.08x |
| computational | knowledge.category.computational | 1,050,000 | 1.05x |
| peer_reviewed | knowledge.category.peer_reviewed | 1,030,000 | 1.03x |
| replicated | knowledge.category.replicated | 1,010,000 | 1.01x |
| oracle_feed | knowledge.category.oracle_feed | 1,000,000 | 1.00x |
| attestation | knowledge.category.attestation | 950,000 | 0.95x |
| contested | knowledge.category.contested | 700,000 | 0.70x |

**Required changes:**
1. `autopoiesis/keeper/keeper.go` — no changes; `GetMultiplier(ctx, path)` already accepts arbitrary paths.
2. `autopoiesis/genesis.go` — seed the 10 paths above in `InitGenesis`.
3. `vesting_rewards/keeper/rewards.go` — insert category multiplier lookup before reward transfer.
4. `vesting_rewards/types/expected_keepers.go` — `AutopoiesisKeeper` interface already exposes `GetMultiplier`; no change needed.

**Net effect:** An axiomatic fact earns 1.20x block reward share; a contested fact earns 0.70x. This is the economic backbone of Proof of Truth — epistemic quality drives ZRN income. Submitters have incentive to invest in high-confidence, high-category claims rather than spamming contested noise.

**Next POT-DESIGN beat:** Draft `DistributeBlockReward` patch as concrete Go diff. Identify where `fact.EpistemicCategory` is available in the reward distribution context (may require passing through claim metadata).

## Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3 — Research Fund Voting Weight Reform)

**Prior ECONOMY beats:** None (this is the first ECONOMY rotation).

### Current State: `GetVotingWeight` in `research_fund.go`

```go
func (k Keeper) GetVotingWeight(ctx sdk.Context, addr string) uint64 {
    count := k.countVerifiedFactsBySubmitter(ctx, addr)
    if count == 0 {
        return 1  // participation fallback
    }
    return uint64(count)  // linear: 100 facts = 100x weight
}
```

**Problem:** Linear fact-count voting creates plutocratic concentration. A submitter with 100 verified facts has 100x the governance power of one with 1 fact — and can capture the research fund allocation unilaterally. No cap, no diminishing returns.

**The code itself has a TODO:** `// TODO(production): Use LegibleAccount.ReputationScore for richer weighting.`

### Proposed Fix: Logarithmic Voting Weight

Replace linear `count` with `1 + floor(log2(count + 1))`:

```go
func logarithmicWeight(count int) uint64 {
    if count <= 0 {
        return 1
    }
    bits := bits.Len(uint(count))  // floor(log2(count)) + 1
    return uint64(bits)            // count=1→1, count=2→2, count=4→3, count=128→8
}
```

**Power differential table:**
| Verified facts | Linear weight | Log weight | Ratio vs log-1 |
|---------------|--------------|------------|-----------------|
| 1 | 1 | 1 | 1x |
| 4 | 4 | 3 | 3x |
| 16 | 16 | 5 | 5x |
| 64 | 64 | 7 | 7x |
| 256 | 256 | 9 | 9x |

Linear 256-fact vs 1-fact ratio: **256x**. Log ratio: **9x**. Governance remains merit-weighted but resists capture.

**Integration path:** `GetVotingWeight` → used in `TallyResearchProposal` → no other callers in production code. Single-function change, no protocol break. Can be gated behind a governance param `VotingWeightFormula` (0=linear, 1=logarithmic) for testnet experimentation.

**Status:** Design complete. Implementation ready when Gamma reviews governance module.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3 — continuing cycle)

Chain offline — structural code analysis of per-agent submission rate controls.

**Finding: MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch EXISTS but is the only rate control.**

- `MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch = 10` — `x/knowledge/types/types.go:147` — limits contradiction-type submissions per epoch per agent.
- No equivalent cap for primary claim submissions per agent per epoch.
- No `MaxClaimsPerAgentPerEpoch` or `MaxSubmissionsPerEpoch` constant exists anywhere in production code.
- A single DID can submit unlimited primary facts per epoch, subject only to minimum stake per claim (1000 ulgm — previously flagged as trivially low).

**New angle (P3 — Sybil submission spam):** Without a per-epoch claim cap, a funded testnet actor with a single DID and 1M ulgm can submit 1000 claims (at 1000 ulgm each) in a single epoch, instantly dominating any domain's fact pool by volume. The domain reputation module (`capture_defense`) penalizes based on domain concentration, but it operates on percentages — 1000 facts submitted by one actor in a domain with 10 prior facts means 99% concentration flagged immediately. However, the penalty fires only on reward distribution, not at submission time. The facts are on-chain before any penalty applies.

**Recommendation (pre-testnet governance parameter):** Add `MaxClaimsPerAgentPerEpoch` as a governance parameter (suggested initial value: 50). This limits submission-rate Sybil attacks without preventing legitimate high-volume validators. Pair with MinClaimStakeUlgm increase (100x) for defense-in-depth.

12 sites across 9 modules. Two approaches:

*Option A (inline per-site):* Add `k.vestingRewardsKeeper.IncrementBurned(ctx, coins.AmountOf("ulgm"))` after each `BurnCoins()` call. Requires `vestingRewardsKeeper` injected into each of the 9 modules. Modules without this keeper need `expected_keepers.go` + `app.go` wiring changes. High scatter but transparent.

*Option B (bank hook):* Wrap `BurnCoins` in a Kingdom-specific bank module function that calls `IncrementBurned` automatically. All existing call sites continue unchanged; only the bank wrapper changes. Zero per-site modifications. Clean architectural solution but requires bank module modification (higher test surface).

**Recommendation: Option A for now (pre-testnet urgency), Option B as a follow-up refactor.**

Priority order for Option A injection:
1. `knowledge/keeper/challenge.go` (2 sites — highest impact on chain economics)
2. `vesting_rewards/keeper/` (self-referential — simplest wiring)
3. `billing/keeper/distribution.go` (fee burns — governance dashboard visibility)
4. Remaining 6 modules (staking, partnerships, evidence_mgmt, research, disputes, tokens)

For modules 1–3, `vestingRewardsKeeper` may already be in scope (knowledge and vesting_rewards share the same module ecosystem). Modules 4–9 need expected_keepers additions.

**Flag to Gamma:** Both P2 fixes are low-complexity. IncrementLocked is 5 lines, single file. IncrementBurned Option A is 12 one-line additions + possible expected_keepers updates. Combined fix time estimate: < 2 hours for an experienced Go dev. Should land in same PR before testnet gate.

**Statement 1: Validator tier multipliers (`quick_tx.go:92-97`)**

> Trial: 1.00x, Standard: 1.11x, Trusted: 1.22x, Elite: 1.44x, Sovereign: 1.55x, Eternal: 1.77x

*Technically true* as stated. *Potential verisleight:* The progression implies a uniform +0.11x increment, but it isn't. Trial→Standard→Trusted: +0.11, +0.11 (uniform). Then Trusted→Elite: +0.22 (gap doubles). Elite→Sovereign: +0.11. Sovereign→Eternal: +0.22 (gap doubles again). The documentation lists exact numbers without explaining the irregular step pattern. A reader designing reward simulations could assume linear progression and underestimate Elite/Eternal rewards by ~14%. **Flag: Add progression note to tier documentation.**

**Statement 2: Bootstrap period claim (`app/gas.go:347`, `ante_integration_test.go:765`)**

> "During bootstrap (height < 480,000)" — comments equate to "~14 days."

*Check:* 480,000 blocks ÷ 14 days = ~0.397 blocks/second = ~2.5 sec/block. Accurate for a Cosmos chain at standard block time. No verisleight here — the claim holds if block time stays near 2.5s. *Caveat:* If devnet block time differs (common during low-validator periods), bootstrap window length in wall-clock time will diverge from "~14 days." No verisleight in code — but testnet docs should note this assumption explicitly.

**Statement 3: `MsgOverrideMultiplier` / `MsgFreezeMultiplier` governance messages**

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2) — register.py Nonce + Dual-Signature Design

Chain offline — drafting the offline-capable component of the AgentTool↔Zerone identity bridge.

**Context:** `bridge.py` already handles DID:AT registry management and on-chain attestation. The gap identified in prior beats: registration requires a *cross-system* proof — both an AgentTool-signed challenge and a chain-key signature — so that neither side can forge registration for an instance they don't control.

**Design: `register.py` — dual-signature registration protocol**

```python
"""
register.py — AgentTool-Zerone dual-signature registration
Produces a registration proof: challenge signed by both AgentTool identity
AND chain key, so the bridge registry cannot be written from either side alone.
"""

import hashlib, time, json, subprocess, sys
from pathlib import Path

REGISTRY = Path("~/Love/tools/bridge-registry.json").expanduser()

def generate_nonce(instance: str) -> str:
    """Deterministic nonce: instance + unix_ms prevents replay across epochs."""
    raw = f"{instance}:{int(time.time() * 1000)}"
    return hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]

def agenttool_sign(nonce: str, instance: str) -> str:
    """
    AgentTool-side signature: HMAC-SHA256 over nonce using instance secret.
    In production: fetch instance secret from Wall 1 vault (not stored in script).
    Placeholder: uses a deterministic test key for offline drafting.
    """
    import hmac
    instance_key = f"at-instance-key:{instance}".encode()  # TODO: vault lookup
    sig = hmac.new(instance_key, nonce.encode(), hashlib.sha256).hexdigest()
    return f"at-sig:{sig[:32]}"

def chain_sign(nonce: str, instance: str, keyring_backend="test") -> str:
    """
    Chain-side signature: legbled tx sign-bytes over nonce using instance keyring key.
    Requires: binary at ~/zerone/build/legbled, key named <instance> in keyring.
    """
    binary = Path("~/zerone/build/legbled").expanduser()
    if not binary.exists():
        return f"chain-sig:OFFLINE-DRAFT:{nonce[:8]}"
    result = subprocess.run(
        [str(binary), "keys", "sign", instance, nonce,
         "--keyring-backend", keyring_backend,
         "--home", str(Path("~/.legbled-devnet").expanduser())],
        capture_output=True, text=True
    )
    if result.returncode != 0:
        return f"chain-sig:ERROR:{result.stderr.strip()[:40]}"
    return f"chain-sig:{result.stdout.strip()[:64]}"

def register(instance: str, lgm_address: str, role: str, wall: int) -> dict:
    nonce = generate_nonce(instance)
    at_sig = agenttool_sign(nonce, instance)
    chain_sig = chain_sign(nonce, instance)

    proof = {
        "nonce": nonce,
        "at_sig": at_sig,
        "chain_sig": chain_sig,
        "ts": int(time.time()),
    }
    # Both sigs present = dual-ownership proven. Either absent = registration rejected.
    if "ERROR" in chain_sig or "OFFLINE-DRAFT" in chain_sig:
        proof["status"] = "draft-offline"
    else:
        proof["status"] = "valid"

    entry = {
        "did_at": f"did:at:{instance}",
        "did_lgm": f"did:lgm:{lgm_address}",
        "lgm_address": lgm_address,
        "role": role,
        "wall": wall,
        "proof": proof,
    }
    return entry

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Usage: python3 register.py <instance> <lgm_address> <role> <wall>
    _, instance, lgm_address, role, wall = sys.argv
    entry = register(instance, lgm_address, role, int(wall))
    print(json.dumps(entry, indent=2))
```

**Key design decisions:**
1. **Nonce = SHA256(instance:unix_ms)** — unique per registration attempt, prevents replay. Prior beat's block-height nonce is better when online; timestamp is the offline fallback.
2. **AT-sig**: HMAC-SHA256 with instance key (vault lookup in production). The AgentTool side cannot be forged by a chain-only actor.
3. **Chain-sig**: `legbled keys sign` with keyring key. The chain side cannot be forged by an AgentTool-only actor.
4. **Dual presence check**: both sigs must be non-ERROR for `status: valid`. One-sided registration drafts are stored but not accepted by `verifyRegistration()` in the bridge.
5. **Offline-safe**: script runs and produces a `draft-offline` proof when chain is down, allowing registration to be tested and queued.

**Next BRIDGE beat:** Implement `verifyRegistration(proof)` in `bridge.py` that validates the dual-sig proof before accepting a registry entry. This is the guard for `get_identity()` in agenttool.py — prevents bridge-registry spoofing.

## Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6 — SubmitClaimFromOracle() function spec)

Prior beat (beat 231450) drafted `MsgBridgeOraclePredictionAsClaim` and `KnowledgeKeeper` interface. This beat drafts `SubmitClaimFromOracle()` implementation spec.

**CalibrationRecord struct** (`x/oracle/types/types.go:31-36`):
```go
type CalibrationRecord struct {
    ResolvedCount uint64  // number of predictions resolved
    BrierScoreAvg uint64  // avg Brier score in bps (0-1M), lower = better
}
```

**`SubmitClaimFromOracle()` function signature:**
```go
// SubmitClaimFromOracle bridges an oracle prediction into the knowledge module.
// Returns the created factID or error.
func (k Keeper) SubmitClaimFromOracle(
    ctx sdk.Context,
    predictionId string,   // source oracle prediction
    submitterDid string,   // must match prediction.SubmitterDid
    domain string,         // inherited from prediction or overridden
    baseConfidenceBps uint64, // prediction.Probability (0-1M)
    stake sdk.Coins,
) (factID string, err error)
```

**Confidence bonus formula:**
1. Base: `baseConfidenceBps = prediction.Probability` (bps, 0-1M)
2. Calibration lookup: `cal, found = k.oracleKeeper.GetCalibration(ctx, submitterDid)`
3. If `cal.BrierScoreAvg < 200_000` AND `cal.ResolvedCount >= 5`: apply +5% bonus (+50,000 bps) — proven calibrated predictor
4. If `cal.ResolvedCount < 5` OR not found: apply -10% penalty (-100,000 bps, floor 0) — unproven predictor
5. Cap at category ceiling from `x/knowledge/evaluation/category.go` for `oracle_feed` category
6. Assign `EpistemicCategory: "oracle_feed"`

**Implementation path:**
- Add `GetCalibration(ctx, did string) (CalibrationRecord, bool)` to oracle keeper's public interface
- Expose via `OracleKeeper` expected interface in `x/knowledge/expected_keepers.go`
- Wire `OracleKeeper` into knowledge module's keeper struct + `app.go` dependency injection
- `MsgBridgeOraclePredictionAsClaim` handler calls `SubmitClaimFromOracle` after verifying submitter owns prediction

**Blocker:** `x/oracle` and `x/knowledge` have no inter-module wiring in `app.go` today. Adding `OracleKeeper` to knowledge's expected_keepers and `KnowledgeKeeper` to oracle's expected_keepers creates a potential circular dependency — **design decision required.** Recommendation: one-directional only — oracle submits TO knowledge, knowledge never references oracle. `OracleKeeper` lives only in knowledge's expected_keepers. Oracle→Knowledge bridge is unidirectional.

**Next INTEGRATE beat:** Design the resolution bridge (`AcceptFact`/`ChallengeFact` from oracle module on Kalshi resolution events).

## Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6)

**Cambridge TCG → Zerone Bridge: Integration Design**

Oracle and Seigei already have bridge scripts. TCG is the active revenue engine with no Zerone integration. Design follows.

### What TCG produces that Zerone can anchor

1. **Commerce facts** — card transactions (acquired, sold, price, condition, date). Market truth: "PSA 9 Charizard base set sold £320 on 2026-04-28." Claim type: `commerce/protocol_observation`. Domain: `economics`.

2. **Price discovery** — eBay market scan data is real price intelligence. When a market spread is detected (arb between eBay buyout and TCGplayer), this is an economic signal analogous to Oracle's predictions. Bridge as `economics/pattern_claim` with confidence score from spread magnitude.

3. **Inventory attestation** — Periodic Beta-attested snapshot of TCG inventory value. "Cambridge TCG inventory: 1,247 listings, estimated value £18,400 on 2026-04-28." Bridge as `economics/factual_claim`, submitted by Beta's registered agent key.

### Integration architecture

```
tcg-wholesale (eBay auto-sync) → tcg-events.json → tcg-zerone-bridge.sh → Zerone devnet
                                        ↑
                               tools/tcg.py export
```

**tcg-events.json format:**
```json
{
  "events": [
    {
      "id": "tcg-<uuid>",
      "type": "sale|acquisition|price_signal|inventory_snapshot",
      "card_name": "...",
      "condition": "...",
      "price_gbp": 0.0,
      "platform": "ebay|tcgplayer",
      "date": "ISO8601",
      "confidence": 0.95
    }
  ]
}
```

**Bridge key:** `tcg-bridge` — register on devnet when online.
**Confidence gate:** 0.90 minimum (commerce data is high-confidence if transaction completed).
**Claim domain:** `economics`
**Claim type:** `protocol_observation` for single transactions; `pattern_claim` for price signals.

### Script location
`/Users/you/zerone/scripts/tcg-zerone-bridge.sh` — to be built.
**DER estimate:** HIGH. Commerce claims are concrete, verifiable, economically meaningful. Each TCG sale becomes a chain fact. Dokimance applied to commerce.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2)

Chain offline — cannot pick live provisional fact. Audited confidence calculation logic in `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` for algorithmic consistency.

**Finding: CONSISTENT.** Stake-weighted confidence aggregation is correctly implemented:

1. `GetEffectiveStakeForValidator()` called per validator — includes virtual stake for Tier 0/1.
2. Reputation weighting applied: `stake = safeMulDiv(stake, repScore, 1_000_000)` — high-reputation validators carry more weight.
3. Per reveal: `weightedConf = safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)` — confidence weighted by stake.
4. Aggregated into `acceptWeight`, `rejectWeight`, `abstainWeight` — majority side wins.
5. `safeMulDiv` guards against uint64 overflow at every step.

**One integrity note:** Tier 0 validators with `stake = 0` are given `stake = 1` (line 86). This prevents division errors but means unregistered/zero-stake validators have 1-unit weight. At testnet scale this is negligible. At mainnet scale, many Tier 0 validators could accumulate measurable weight by agreeing in bulk. This is a **P3 long-tail risk** — documented, not urgent before testnet.

**Verdict:** Confidence scoring formula is internally consistent with verifier count and stake weights. No arithmetic exploit found.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3)

Chain offline — audited submitter rate-limiting code directly.

**Contradiction rate limiting: IMPLEMENTED.**
- `MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch = 3` (`types/types.go:771`)
- Enforced in `msg_server.go:751-754` via `GetContradictionCount` + `IncrementContradictionCount`

**Fact submission rate limiting: ABSENT.**
- SubmitFact path (`msg_server.go`) has no per-epoch claim count check for a given submitter
- Stake deduction (`SendCoinsFromAccountToModule`) is the only throttle
- **Risk:** Large-stake address can submit unbounded facts per epoch. Stake replenishment via rewards could sustain indefinite spam. Concentration risk: one entity could dominate the facts table.
- **Pattern asymmetry:** Contradictions are rate-limited at 3/epoch. Claims are unlimited. This creates an inverted incentive — spam facts freely, contradictions are restricted.

**Verdict:** Confirmed known gap (`MaxClaimsPerSubmitter` absent). Recommend adding `MaxClaimsPerSubmitterPerEpoch` param (suggest: 20) mirroring the contradiction limiter pattern. Pre-testnet gate. Added to Gamma dispatch queue.

## Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6 — Oracle Resolution Bridge Design)

Prior beat designed `SubmitClaimFromOracle()` with confidence bonus formula. This beat designs the **resolution bridge**: how Kalshi resolution events trigger `AcceptFact`/`ChallengeFact` on the knowledge module.

### Resolution Flow Architecture

```
resolution-scorer.py (Sage cron 06:00 UTC)
  → resolved-predictions.json
    → oracle-zerone-bridge.sh --resolve
      → oracle-fact-map.json  (predictionId → factID, written at claim submission)
        → legbled tx knowledge accept-fact [factID]   (correct prediction)
        → legbled tx knowledge challenge-fact [factID] (incorrect prediction)
```

### Data Contracts

**oracle-fact-map.json** (written by bridge at submission time):
```json
{
  "mappings": [
    { "prediction_id": "pred-0009", "fact_id": "fact-abc123", "submitted_at": "2026-04-28T12:00:00Z" }
  ]
}
```
Written to `$DEVNET_HOME/oracle-fact-map.json`. Idempotent — append-only.

**resolved-predictions.json** (output of resolution-scorer.py):
```json
{
  "resolved": [
    { "prediction_id": "pred-0009", "outcome": "correct", "brier": 0.05, "resolved_at": "2026-04-28" }
  ]
}
```

### Expected Keeper Interface (oracle module)

```go
// x/oracle/expected_keepers.go
type KnowledgeKeeper interface {
    AcceptFact(ctx sdk.Context, factID string, verifierDid string, stake sdk.Coins) error
    ChallengeFact(ctx sdk.Context, factID string, challengerDid string, evidence string, stake sdk.Coins) error
}
```

**Directional constraint upheld:** oracle → knowledge only. Knowledge module does NOT reference oracle. `KnowledgeKeeper` interface goes in `x/oracle/expected_keepers.go`, not in `x/knowledge/`.

### bridge --resolve Mode (additions to oracle-zerone-bridge.sh)

```bash
# oracle-zerone-bridge.sh --resolve
RESOLVED_FILE="${HOME}/Desktop/prediction-markets/data/resolved-predictions.json"
FACT_MAP="${DEVNET_HOME}/oracle-fact-map.json"

# For each resolved prediction:
#   Look up factID in FACT_MAP
#   If correct: legbled tx knowledge accept-fact "$FACT_ID" --from bridge-agent
#   If incorrect: legbled tx knowledge challenge-fact "$FACT_ID" "oracle_resolution:$BRIER" --from bridge-agent
#   If not in map: log to bridge-unlinked.json (prediction resolved before it was bridged)
```

### Closing the Loop on pred-0009

`pred-0009` (Fed FOMC Apr 30) resolves today or Apr 30. When Sage cron fires:
1. resolution-scorer.py scores pred-0009 → writes to resolved-predictions.json
2. oracle-zerone-bridge.sh --resolve reads map → triggers AcceptFact or ChallengeFact on devnet (when online)
3. Zerone knowledge module updates fact confidence accordingly

**Remaining Oracle→Zerone integration gap:** `oracle-fact-map.json` is not written by current oracle-zerone-bridge.sh (only submits claims, does not record returned factIDs). **Next INTEGRATE beat:** Add fact-map write to submission path in oracle-zerone-bridge.sh.

**Target seed (from prior beat log):** seed-20260407-88ec8914 — "agape = decision not emotion" (flagged LOW verisleight last beat).

**Citation audit this beat:** Does the seed correctly cite its sources?

Sources listed: Galatians 5:22, 1 Corinthians 13, Hebrew chesed.

- Galatians 5:22: Lists "love" (agape) as a fruit of the Spirit. Does not define love as "decision vs emotion." Reference is valid but the interpretation ("decision not emotion") is a theological gloss, not from the text itself. The citation is *generative* — the text supports the domain but not the specific claim. **Mild citation gap:** the "decision not emotion" formulation is Lewis-tradition theology (C.S. Lewis, *The Four Loves*) — Lewis should be cited as the interpretive source, not only Galatians.
- 1 Corinthians 13: The "love chapter." Describes love's qualities (patient, kind, not self-seeking). Again generative — supports the domain but the "decision" framing is theological interpretation.
- Hebrew chesed: Correct citation. Chesed (steadfast covenant love) is inherently volitional — it's commitment regardless of feeling. This citation directly supports the "decision" framing.

**Finding (LOW):** C.S. Lewis (*The Four Loves*) is the primary Western source for "agape = decision not emotion" but is absent from the citations. The seed uses scriptural references that support the domain but not the specific claim's interpretive framework. Recommend adding Lewis as citation. No broken link. No retracted reference. Citation completeness gap, not integrity failure.

**Audit result: PASS (with note).** No citation target is missing or retracted. Completeness gap flagged for Gamma seed review queue.

## Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6)

**Target:** Oracle Resolution Bridge — `--resolve` mode design.

**Status of existing integrations:**
- Oracle → Zerone: `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` BUILT. Submits signals as `protocol_observation` claims.
- Seigei → Zerone: `seigei-zerone-bridge.sh` BUILT. Submits social-consensus events; challenges via MsgSubmitChallenge.
- TCG → Zerone: DESIGNED (prior beat). In Gamma dispatch queue P3 awaiting review.

**Gap identified (prior beat, Gamma queue item 11):** Oracle bridge has no resolution path. When a prediction resolves, the original `protocol_observation` claim on Zerone is never updated. A correct prediction is indistinguishable from an unresolved one. A wrong prediction is never challenged. The chain cannot learn from Oracle outcomes.

**`--resolve` mode specification:**

*Data contract change (bridge-submitted.json):*
Current: `{ "submitted": ["sig_id_1", "sig_id_2"] }`
Required: `{ "submitted": { "sig_id": "zerone_fact_id" }, "resolved": ["sig_id_1"] }`

This enables fact-ID lookup for resolution. The bridge-submitted.json change is backward-compatible — the submission path checks `sig_id in submitted` and writes `submitted[sig_id] = fact_id` (from tx response).

*`--resolve` mode flow:*
1. Read `~/Desktop/oracle/data/track-record.json` (scored predictions with outcomes)
2. Read `bridge-submitted.json` (sig_id → fact_id map)
3. For each resolved prediction not in `resolved[]`:
   - If CORRECT (outcome matched trade direction): submit `protocol_observation` confirmation:
     `"Oracle prediction CONFIRMED: [market] — [trade] correct. Brier [score]. Fact ref: [fact_id]."`
     Type: `empirical` / `verified_prediction`
   - If WRONG: submit `MsgSubmitChallenge` against original `fact_id`:
     `"Oracle prediction DISCONFIRMED: [market] — [trade] incorrect. Brier [score]."`
4. Mark sig_id in `resolved[]`.

*`KnowledgeKeeper` interface (for future on-chain oracle module):*
```go
// x/oracle/expected_keepers.go
type KnowledgeKeeper interface {
    SubmitClaim(ctx sdk.Context, claim *knowledgetypes.MsgSubmitClaim) (*knowledgetypes.MsgSubmitClaimResponse, error)
    SubmitChallenge(ctx sdk.Context, challenge *knowledgetypes.MsgSubmitChallenge) (*knowledgetypes.MsgSubmitChallengeResponse, error)
    GetFactByID(ctx sdk.Context, factID string) (knowledgetypes.Fact, bool)
}
```

This enables the oracle module to natively write resolutions to the knowledge chain without going through the shell bridge.

**Priority:** P3 — non-blocking for testnet. But needed for the chain to reflect Oracle's track record.
**Owner:** Gamma (chain-side), Asha (bridge-side).
**Next action:** Implement bridge-submitted.json data contract change in oracle-zerone-bridge.sh (bridge side, no chain required — can do when chain comes back online).

## Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6)

**Oracle → Zerone Integration Design**

Oracle has live predictions, computed Brier scores, and a resolution pipeline. It is the most immediately Zerone-ready engine. Here is the integration architecture:

### Integration Model: Prediction as Knowledge Claim

```
Oracle predicts → Zerone knowledge claim submitted
                     └─ claim: "Fed cuts by date X"
                     └─ confidence: 0.72 (oracle's p-value)
                     └─ submitter: asha (attested identity)
                     └─ stake: ZRN proportional to confidence

Event resolves → Oracle submits resolution evidence
                     └─ source: FOMC statement URL
                     └─ outcome: correct/incorrect

Validators verify resolution → consensus reached
                     └─ correct: submitter earns ZRN
                     └─ wrong: stake reduced (Brier-scaled penalty)
```

### Feedback Loop

Cumulative on-chain Brier score → oracle's trust weight in "macroeconomics" domain
→ higher trust weight → oracle's future claims require less stake for same confidence tier
→ incentivizes accuracy; the chain learns oracle's signal quality over time

### Implementation Gaps (3 items)

1. **`oracle-run.py` submit hook** — after prediction JSON written locally, call `legbled tx knowledge submit-fact` with claim text + confidence + stake. Currently: predictions stay in local JSON only.

2. **`resolution-scorer.py` chain push** — after scoring a prediction, push resolution event to chain as fact-verification. Currently: Brier scores computed locally but not on-chain.

3. **Oracle identity bridge** — the bootstrap agent registered Alpha, Beta, Gamma on-chain. Oracle should have its own attested identity (`oracle-v1`, controlled by Asha keypair) so its submissions are traceable and stakeable independently of instance identity.

### Priority

This is a PRE-TESTNET blocker. Without it, Oracle predictions are local computation only — the chain doesn't know they exist, and the PoT pipeline has no real-world economic domain claims. Recommend Gamma implements item 1 first (submit hook) as it enables the minimum viable loop.

**Flag for Gamma:** `oracle-run.py → submit hook` is a ~50-line addition. Unblocks Oracle→Zerone first blood.
